(4.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{*}(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\square^{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\square^{n}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times\square^{\alpha}}

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
Consider the morphism
\alpha:=\delta_{n}\colon[n-1]\to[n]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
with an integer
n\geq 1
.
Then there exists a functor
\alpha^{*}\colon\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n-1}
satisfying the following property:
For
Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
,
there exists a commutative square

(4.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{*}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\square^{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\square^{n}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times\square^{\alpha}}

Proof.

Let
V\to X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
be a dividing cover corresponding to
Y
.
Consider the partial dividing
\alpha^{*}(V)\to X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{m}
obtained by Construction
4.10
,
which is a dividing cover since it is proper.
The morphism (
4.2
) is a proper open immersion and hence an isomorphism.
It follows that the morphism
\alpha^{*}(Y)\to X\times\square^{n-1}
obtained by Construction
4.10
is an admissible blow-up, so we have
\alpha^{*}(Y)\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n-1}
.
We also obtain (
4.4
) from (
4.3
).
The construction of
\alpha^{*}(Y)
is natural, so we obtain a desired functor
\alpha^{*}
.
∎

Definition 4.12
.

Let
\mathrm{SBl}(\mathbb{F}_{1})
be the category of triples
(Y,X,[n])
with
Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
,
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
whose morphisms are of the form

(u,f,\alpha)\colon(Y^{\prime},X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}])\to(Y,X,[n])

such that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}\times\square^{n^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\square^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\times\square^{\alpha}}

commutes.
Equivalently,
the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}-\partial X^{\prime})\times\mathbb{A}^{n^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{A}^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f-\partial f)\times\mathbb{A}^{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

commutes since
(X^{\prime}-\partial X^{\prime})\times\mathbb{A}^{n^{\prime}}
is dense in
Y^{\prime}
.

A morphism
(u,f,\alpha)\colon(Y^{\prime},X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}])\to(Y,X,[n])
is an
admissible blow-up
if
f
and
\alpha
are isomorphisms.
Let
\mathrm{SAdm}
denote the class of admissible blow-ups.

Proposition 4.13
.

The class
\mathrm{SAdm}
in
\mathrm{SBl}(\mathbb{F}_{1})
admits calculus of right fractions.

Proof.

Let

(u,f,\alpha)\colon(Y^{\prime},X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}])\to(Y,X,[n]),\text{ }(v,\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})\colon(Z,X,[n])\to(Y,X,[n])

be morphisms in
\mathrm{SBl}(\mathbb{F}_{1})
so that
(v,\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})\in\mathrm{SAdm}
.
Note that
\alpha
is a composite of several
\delta_{i}\colon[l]\to[l+1]
and
p_{i}\colon[l]\to[l-1]
.
To check the Ore condition,
we may assume that
f=\mathrm{id}
and
\alpha
is one of the above or
f
is arbitrary and
\alpha=\mathrm{id}
.
By Propositions
4.6
and
4.11
and Construction
4.9
,
we have a morphism
(w,f,\alpha)\colon(Z^{\prime},X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}])\to(Z,X,[n])
for some
w
.
Since
\mathrm{SBl}_{X^{\prime}}^{n^{\prime}}
is cofiltered by Proposition
4.7
, replacing
Z^{\prime}
,
we may assume that there exists a morphism
Z^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{SBl}_{X^{\prime}}^{n^{\prime}}
.
The square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}

commutes since
(X^{\prime}-\partial X^{\prime})\times\mathbb{A}^{n^{\prime}}
is dense in
Z^{\prime}
.
This shows the Ore condition.

For the right cancellability condition,
let
(u_{1},f_{1},\alpha_{1}),(u_{2},f_{2},\alpha_{2})\colon(Y^{\prime},X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}])\to(Y,X,[n])
be two morphisms, and let
(v,\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})\colon(Y^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}])\to(Y^{\prime},X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}])
be an admissible blow-up such that

(u_{1},f_{1},\alpha_{1})\circ(v,\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})=(u_{2},f_{2},\alpha_{2})\circ(v,\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id}).

Then we have
f_{1}=f_{2}
and
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}
,
and the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}-\partial X^{\prime})\times\mathbb{A}^{n^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{A}^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f-\partial f)\times\mathbb{A}^{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{i}}

commutes for
i=1,2
.
Since
(X^{\prime}-\partial X^{\prime})\times\mathbb{A}^{n^{\prime}}
is dense in
Y^{\prime}
,
we have
u_{1}=u_{2}
.
This shows the right cancellability condition.
∎

Proposition 4.14
.

The functor

\mathrm{SBl}(\mathbb{F}_{1})[\mathrm{SAdm}^{-1}]\to\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}\times\mathbf{\Delta}

induced by the forgetful functor
\mathrm{SBl}(\mathbb{F}_{1})\to\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}\times\mathbf{\Delta}

is an equivalence of categories.

Proof.

The inclusion functor
\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}\times\mathbf{\Delta}\to\mathrm{SBl}(\mathbb{F}_{1})
given by

(X,[n])\mapsto(X\times\square^{n},X,[n])

induces an essentially surjective functor

\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}\times\mathbf{\Delta}\to\mathrm{SBl}(\mathbb{F}_{1})[\mathrm{SAdm}^{-1}].

It suffices to show that this is fully faithful.
Let
(X,[n]),(X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}])\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}\times\mathbf{\Delta}
,
and let
(Y^{\prime},X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}])\to(X^{\prime}\times\square^{n^{\prime}},X^{\prime},n^{\prime})
be an admissible blow-up.
Then we have

\Hom_{\mathrm{SBl}(\mathbb{F}_{1})}((Y^{\prime},X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}]),(X\times\square^{n},X,[n]))\simeq\Hom_{\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}\times\mathbf{\Delta}}((X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}]),(X,[n])),

which finishes the proof.
∎

Definition 4.15
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a presheaf of spectra on
\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
. Consider the presheaf of spectra
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathrm{SBl}(\mathbb{F}_{1})
given by
\mathcal{F}(Y,X,[n]):=\mathcal{F}(Y)
.
Note that
\mathrm{SAdm}
admits a calculus of right fractions by Proposition
4.13
.
Argue as in
[
56
, Lemma 4.3]
to obtain an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SBl}(\mathbb{F}_{1})[\mathrm{SAdm}^{-1}],\mathrm{Sp})\simeq\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SBl}(\mathbb{F}_{1}),\mathrm{Sp})[\mathrm{SAdm}^{-1}].

Let

L_{\mathrm{SAdm}}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SBl}(\mathbb{F}_{1}),\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SBl}(\mathbb{F}_{1}),\mathrm{Sp})[\mathrm{SAdm}^{-1}]

be the localization functor.
Together with Proposition
4.14
,
we obtain

\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}_{\bullet}\mathcal{F}:=L_{\mathrm{SAdm}}\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}\times\mathbf{\Delta},\mathrm{Sp}).

Let
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}_{n}\mathcal{F}(X)
denote its value at
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
and
n\in\mathbf{\Delta}
.
Then we have

\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}_{n}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(Y)

for
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
and integer
n\geq 0
.
We define

(4.5)

\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}:=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}_{n}\mathcal{F}\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(Y).

The construction of
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}
is natural in
\mathcal{F}
,
so we obtain a functor

\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1},\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1},\mathrm{Sp})

Remark 4.16
.

For an integer
n\geq 0
,
let
\mathrm{Sdiv}^{n}(\mathbb{F}_{1})
be the category of pairs
(V,X)
with
V\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
and
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
whose morphisms
(h,f)\colon(V^{\prime},X^{\prime})\to(V,X)
are the commutative squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}.}

Let
\mathrm{SAdm}
be the class of morphisms
(h,f)\colon(V^{\prime},X^{\prime})\to(V,X)
in
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
such that
f
is an isomorphism.
Then argue as in Proposition
4.14
to obtain an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{Sdiv}^{n}(\mathbb{F}_{1})[\mathrm{SAdm}^{-1}]\simeq\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a presheaf of spectra on
\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
Consider the full subcategory
\mathrm{SBl}^{n}(\mathbb{F}_{1})
of
\mathrm{SBl}(\mathbb{F}_{1})
whose entries for
\mathbf{\Delta}
are
[n]
,
and consider the presheaf of spectra
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathrm{SBl}^{n}(\mathbb{F}_{1})
given by
\mathcal{F}(Y,X,[n]):=\mathcal{F}(Y)
.
Argue as in Proposition
4.4
to show that
\mathrm{SBl}^{n}(\mathbb{F}_{1})
is a full subcategory of
\mathrm{Sdiv}^{n}(\mathbb{F}_{1})
.
Let
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Sdiv}}
be its left Kan extension to
\mathrm{Sdiv}^{n}(\mathbb{F}_{1})
.
Argue as in Definition
4.15
also to obtain

L_{\mathrm{SAdm}}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Sdiv}}\in\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1},\mathrm{Sp}),

which agrees with
\widetilde{\log}_{n}\mathcal{F}
since the functor
\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
is cofinal by Proposition
4.8
.
Hence we have an alternative description

(4.6)

\widetilde{\log}_{n}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Sdiv}}(Y).

This is sometimes useful because for every morphism
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
in
\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
,
we have the explicit functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X^{\prime}}^{n}
in Construction
4.9
.

Definition 4.17
.

The
logarithm functor
is

\mathrm{\ell og}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\omega^{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{F}_{1},\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1},\mathrm{Sp}),

where
\omega^{\sharp}
is the left Kan extension functor

\omega^{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{F}_{1},\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1},\mathrm{Sp}).

Explicitly,
for a presheaf
\mathcal{F}
of spectra on
\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
and
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
,
(
4.5
) yields a natural isomorphism

(4.7)

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(\underline{Y}).

Observe also that we have a natural morphism

\mathcal{F}(X)\to\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X).

Our next purpose is to define the cubical version of the logarithm functor.

Proposition 4.18
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
For all integers
n\geq 1
and
1\leq i\leq n
,
there exists a functor
\mu_{i}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n+1}
satisfying the following property:
For
V\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
,
there exists a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{i}^{*}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mu_{i}}}

where
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mu_{i}}
is the
i
th multiplication

\mathbb{G}_{m}^{i-1}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{2}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n-i}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}\times\mu\times\mathrm{id}}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{i-1}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n-i}

induced by the multiplication
\mu\colon\mathbb{G}_{m}^{2}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

Proof.

We refer to Construction
3.9
for
\mu^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{Bl}_{(0,\infty)+(\infty,0)}(\square^{2})\to\square
.
Consider
D\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
such that
\underline{D}:=\underline{\mathrm{Bl}_{(0,\infty)+(\infty,0)}(\square^{2})}
and
D-\partial D:=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{2}
.
We have the morphism
\mu^{\prime\prime}\colon D\to\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log}
extending the multiplication morphism
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{2}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
,
and we have
\underline{\mu^{\prime\prime}}=\underline{\mu^{\prime}}
.
Let
g\colon V\to X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
be the dividing cover corresponding to
Y\to X\times\square^{n}
,
and let
g^{\prime}\colon V^{\prime}\to X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n+1}
be its pullback along the morphism

\mathrm{id}\times\mu^{\prime\prime}\times\mathrm{id}\colon(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{i-1}\times D\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n-1-i}\to(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{i-1}\times\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n-1-i}.

Observe that
g^{\prime}
is a dividing cover by Proposition
C.28
since
\mathrm{id}\times\mu^{\prime\prime}\times\mathrm{id}
is a morphism of the form
\mathbb{T}_{\Delta}\to\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}
for some subdivision of fans
\Delta\to\Sigma
.
We have

g^{-1}((X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n})\simeq(X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n},

which implies

g^{\prime-1}((X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n+1})\simeq(X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n+1}.

Hence we have
V^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n+1}
.
The construction of
V^{\prime}
is natural, so we have a functor
\mu_{i}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n+1}
such that
\mu_{i}^{*}(V):=V^{\prime}
,
and
\mu_{i}^{*}
satisfies the desired property.
∎

Definition 4.19
.

Let
\mathrm{SBl}^{\mathrm{cube}}(\mathbb{F}_{1})
be the category of triples
(Y,X,\underline{\mathbf{1}}^{n})
with
Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
,
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
whose morphisms are of the form

(u,f,\alpha)\colon(Y^{\prime},X^{\prime},\underline{\mathbf{1}}^{n^{\prime}})\to(Y,X,\underline{\mathbf{1}}^{n})

such that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}-\partial X^{\prime})\times\mathbb{A}^{n^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{A}^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f-\partial f)\times\mathbb{A}^{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

commutes.

A morphism
(u,f,\alpha)\colon(Y^{\prime},X^{\prime},\underline{\mathbf{1}}^{n^{\prime}})\to(Y,X,\underline{\mathbf{1}}^{n})
is an
admissible blow-up
if
X^{\prime}\to X
and
\underline{\mathbf{1}}^{n^{\prime}}\to\underline{\mathbf{1}}^{n}
are isomorphisms.
Let
\mathrm{SAdm}
denote the class of admissible blow-ups.

Definition 4.20
.

Argue as in Propositions
4.14
and
4.13
but use Proposition
4.18
also to obtain an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{SBl}^{\mathrm{cube}}(\mathbb{F}_{1})[\mathrm{SAdm}^{-1}]\simeq\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}\times\mathrm{ECube}.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a presheaf of complexes on
\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
Consider the presheaf of complexes
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathrm{SBl}^{\mathrm{cube}}(\mathbb{F}_{1})
given by
\mathcal{F}(Y,X,\underline{\mathbf{1}}^{n}):=\mathcal{F}(Y)
for
Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
,
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Argue as in Definition
4.15
to obtain

\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}_{\bullet}\mathcal{F}:=L_{\mathrm{SAdm}}\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}\times\mathrm{ECube},\mathrm{Sp}).

Observe that we have a natural isomorphism

(4.8)

\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}_{n}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}_{n}\mathcal{F}(X)

for all
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}
be the presheaf of complexes on
\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
given by

\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}(X):=\lvert\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}_{\bullet}\mathcal{F}(X)\rvert_{\mathrm{cube}}\simeq\big\lvert\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(Y)\big\rvert_{\mathrm{cube}}

for
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
,
where
\lvert-\rvert_{\mathrm{cube}}
is the cubical geometric realization in Construction
3.4
.
The construction of
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}
is natural in
\mathcal{F}
,
so we obtain a functor

\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1},\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1},\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z})).

Definition 4.21
.

The
cubical logarithm functor
is

\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}\omega^{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{F}_{1},\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1},\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z})).

Explicitly,
for a presheaf
\mathcal{F}
of complexes on
\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
and
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
,
we have

(4.9)

\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}(X):=\lvert\mathrm{\ell og}_{\bullet}\mathcal{F}(X)\rvert_{\mathrm{cube}}=\big\lvert\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(\underline{Y})\big\rvert_{\mathrm{cube}}

using Proposition
4.8
.
Observe also that we have a natural morphism

\mathcal{F}(X)\to\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}(X).

Example 4.22
.

Assume
X=\square^{r}
with an integer
r\geq 0
.
Let
1\leq i\leq n
be integers,
and consider the morphisms
\delta_{i,0},\delta_{i,1}\colon\underline{\mathbf{1}}^{n-1}\to\underline{\mathbf{1}}^{n}
.

Let
Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
,
and consider the fan
\Sigma
corresponding to
Y
.
We have the functor
\delta_{i,0}^{*}\colon\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n-1}
as in Proposition
4.11
.
By Lemma
B.38
,

\underline{\delta_{i.0}^{*}(Y)}
corresponds to the fan
V(e_{r+i})
if
\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+n}\}
denotes the standard coordinates in
\square^{r+n}
.

On the other hand,
we have the functor
\delta_{i,1}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n-1}
as in Construction
4.9
.
Consider
V\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
corresponding to
Y
.
Then
\underline{\delta_{i.1}^{*}(V)}
corresponds to the fan that is the restriction of
\Sigma
to the lattice
\mathbb{Z}^{r+i-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{Z}^{n-i}
since
(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{r+i-1}\times\{1\}\times(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{n}
can be identified with the restriction of the fan
(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{n+r}
to the lattice
\mathbb{Z}^{r+i-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{Z}^{n-i}
.
It follows that we have
\delta_{i,1}^{*}(V)\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
,
so we have an induced functor
\delta_{i,1}^{*}\colon\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n-1}
.

In the remaining part of this section,
we discuss several properties of
\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}
and
\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}
.

Proposition 4.23
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a presheaf of spectra on
\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
Then
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}_{n}\mathcal{F}
is dividing invariant for every integer
n\geq 0
.

Proof.

Let
X^{\prime}\to X
be a dividing cover in
\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
Then we have the functor
f_{!}\colon\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X^{\prime}}^{n}\to\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
given as follows.
Let
V^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X^{\prime}}^{n}
.
Then the composite
V^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}\to X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
is a dividing cover,
and hence we have
V^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
.
This construction is natural, so we obtain
f_{!}
.

Together with the description of the functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X^{\prime}}^{n}
in Construction
4.9
,
we see that
f_{!}
is left adjoint to
f^{*}
.
Since
X^{\prime}\simeq X^{\prime}\times_{X}X^{\prime}
,
we also have
\mathrm{id}\simeq f^{*}f_{!}
.
Using the counit
f_{!}f^{*}\to\mathrm{id}
,
we see that
f_{!}
is cofinal.
Consider the morphisms

\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y^{\prime}\in(\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X^{\prime}}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Sdiv}}(Y^{\prime})\xrightarrow{v}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Sdiv}}(Y)\xrightarrow{u}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y^{\prime}\in(\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X^{\prime}}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Sdiv}}(Y^{\prime})

induced by
f_{!}
and
f^{*}
.
Since
f_{!}
is cofinal,
v
is an isomorphism.
Furthermore,

uv
is obtained by taking the colimits of the identity morphisms,
so
uv
is an isomorphism.
It follows that
u
is an isomorphism,
which implies the claim together with (
4.6
).
∎

Lemma 4.24
.

Let
j\colon X^{\prime}\to X
be an open immersion in
\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
Then the functor
j^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X^{\prime}}^{n}
is essentially surjective and hence cofinal for every integer
n\geq 0
.

Proof.

Let
V^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X^{\prime}}^{n}
.
We have the induced morphism
V^{\prime\sharp}\to X^{\prime\sharp}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
,
see Construction
C.25
for
X^{\prime\sharp}
and
V^{\prime\sharp}
.
For the local case
X=\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{e}\times\mathbb{A}^{m}
and
X^{\prime}=\mathbb{A}{N}^{d^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{e^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{A}^{m^{\prime}}
with integers
0\leq d^{\prime}\leq d
,
0\leq e\leq e^{\prime}
, and
0\leq m^{\prime}\leq m
such that
d+e+m=d^{\prime}+e^{\prime}+m^{\prime}
,
we have
X^{\sharp}\simeq\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{e+m}
and
X^{\prime\sharp}\simeq\mathbb{A}{N}^{d^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{e^{\prime}+m^{\prime}}
.
There exists a dividing cover
W^{\prime}\to\mathbb{A}{N}^{d^{\prime}}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
such that
V^{\prime}\simeq W^{\prime}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{e^{\prime}+m^{\prime}}
.
Since
(X^{\prime}-\partial X^{\prime})\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n}
is an open subscheme of
V^{\prime}
,

\mathbb{G}_{m}^{d^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n}
is an open subscheme of
W^{\prime}
.
Consider the induced dividing cover
V:=\mathbb{A}{N}^{d-d^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{e}\times\mathbb{A}^{m}\times W^{\prime}\to X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
.
Then
(X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n}
is an open subscheme of
V
,
so we have
V\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
.
Furthermore, we have
j^{*}Y\simeq Y^{\prime}
.

The above local construction of
V
is natural, so we can glue the local constructions to construct
V
for general
X
such that
j^{*}V\simeq V^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 4.25
.

Let
p\colon X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to X
be the projection with
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
Then the functor
p^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{n}
is an equivalence of categories for every integer
n\geq 0
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the categories of dividing covers of
X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
and
X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
are equivalent.
In the local case
X=\mathbb{A}_{P}\times\mathbb{A}^{m}
with a toric monoid
P
and integer
m\geq 0
, any dividing cover of
X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
(resp.
X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
) is of the form
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}\times\mathbb{A}^{m}
(resp.
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{m}
) for a subdivision
\Sigma
of
\mathbb{A}_{P}\times(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{n}
.
Hence the claim holds.
In particular,
for every dividing cover
W\to X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
,
we can naturally construct a dividing cover
V\to X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
such that
W\simeq V\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
.

For the general case, glue the local constructions to conclude.
∎

Proposition 4.26
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a presheaf of spectra (resp. complexes) on
\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
If
\mathcal{F}
is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant,
then
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}
(resp.
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}
) is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant.
If
\mathcal{F}
is a Zariski sheaf,
then
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}
(resp.
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}
) is a Zariski sheaf.

Proof.

Assume that
\mathcal{F}
is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant.
Then (
4.6
) and Lemma
4.25
implies that
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}
(resp.
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}
) is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant.

Assume that
\mathcal{F}
is a Zariski sheaf.
Let

Q:=\hbox to81.13pt{\vbox to46.31pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 40.56242pt\lower-23.15279pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

be a Zariski distinguished square in
\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
To show that
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}
(resp.
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}
) is a Zariski sheaf,
we need to show that
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}(Q)
(resp.
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}(Q)
) is cocartesian.
For this,
it suffices to show that the square
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}_{n}(Q)
is cartesian for every integer
n\geq 0
by (
4.8
).
This square can be identified with the induced square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Sdiv}}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Sdiv}}(Y\times_{X}U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Sdiv}}(Y\times_{X}V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{Sdiv}}(Y\times_{X}W)}

by (
4.6
) and Lemma
4.24
,
which is cocartesian since
\mathcal{F}
is a Zariski sheaf.
∎

As a consequence of Propositions
4.23
and
4.26
,
we obtain an induced functor

(4.10)

\mathrm{\ell og}\colon\mathrm{SH}_{S^{1}}(\mathbb{F}_{1})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Zar}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1},\mathrm{Sp})[(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{-1},\mathrm{div}^{-1}].

A
smooth toric monoid
P
is an fs monoid
P
such that
\overline{P}:=P/P^{*}
is free and
P^{\mathrm{gp}}
is torsion free.
In this case, there is an isomorphism
P\simeq\mathbb{N}^{r}\times\mathbb{Z}^{s}
for some integers
r,s\geq 0
.
Let
\mathrm{Adm}_{\mathbb{A}_{P}\times\square^{n}}
denote the category of admissible blow-ups of
\mathbb{A}_{P}\times\square^{n}
in
\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.

Lemma 4.27
.

Let
P
be a smooth toric monoid.
Then for every integer
n\geq 0
,
there is an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}^{n}\simeq\mathrm{Adm}_{\mathbb{A}_{P}\times\square^{n}}.

Proof.

Let
f\colon Y\to\mathbb{A}_{P}\times\square^{n}
be an admissible blow-up.
Then
\underline{f}
is proper birational, so
\underline{f}
is associated with a subdivision of fans
\Delta\to\Sigma
as noted in Example
B.32
.
We have the dividing cover
g\colon\mathbb{T}_{\Delta}\to\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}\simeq\mathbb{A}_{P}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
such that
\underline{g}
is identified with
\underline{f}
.
Hence the inclusion
\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}^{n}\subset\mathrm{Adm}_{\mathbb{A}_{P}\times\square^{n}}
is an equivalence of categories.
∎

Lemma 4.28
.

Let
P
be a smooth toric monoid,
and let
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
If
\mathcal{F}
is a presheaf of complexes on
\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
,
then we have natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{A}_{P}\times\mathbb{A}^{n})\simeq\mathrm{Sing}^{\square}L_{\mathrm{Adm}}\underline{\Hom}(\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{A}^{n}],\mathcal{F})(\mathbb{A}_{P}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{A}_{P}\times\mathbb{A}^{n})\simeq\mathrm{CSing}^{\square}L_{\mathrm{Adm}}\underline{\Hom}(\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{A}^{n}],\mathcal{F})(\mathbb{A}_{P}),

where
\underline{\Hom}
denotes the internal hom.

Proof.

Compare (
3.4
) and (
3.5
) with (
4.7
) and (
4.9
),
and use Lemma
4.27
.
∎

Definition 4.29
.

A presheaf
\mathcal{F}
of spectra on
\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
is
logarithmic
if

\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X\times\square^{n})

for all
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
and integer
n\geq 0
.

A presheaf
\mathcal{F}
of complexes on
\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
is
cubically logarithmic
if

\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}(X\times\square^{n})

for all
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
and integer
n\geq 0
.

Proposition 4.30
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a Zariski sheaf of complexes on
\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
Then we have a natural isomorphism of presheaves of complexes

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}.

In particular,

\mathcal{F}
is logarithmic if and only if it is cubically logarithmic.

Proof.

We need to show
\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}(X)
for
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
By Proposition
4.26
,
we can work Zariski locally on
X
.
Hence we may assume
X=\mathbb{A}_{P}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}
for some smooth toric monoid
P
and integer
n\geq 0
.
Use (
3.3
) and Lemma
4.28
to conclude.
∎

5.
Logarithm functor: Case of schemes

In this section,
we define the logarithm functor
\mathrm{\ell og}
for schemes by adapting Definition
4.17
.
The functor
\mathrm{\ell og}
makes a sheaf of spectra
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathrm{Rg}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
) into a sheaf of spectra on
\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
).
We can view
\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}
as a natural logarithmic extension of
\mathcal{F}
if
\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X)
for every
X\in\mathrm{Rg}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/S
).

The following two definitions are direct analogs of Definition
4.1
and
4.2
.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
).
For
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
be the category of admissible blow-ups
f\colon Y\to X\times\square^{n}
in
\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
) such that there exists a commutative square

(5.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\square^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

in
\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
)
with a dividing cover
g
satisfying
\underline{g}=\underline{f}
,
where
X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}\to X\times\square^{n}
is the canonical map whose underlying morphism of schemes is the identity.
Observe that every morphism in
\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
is an admissible blow-up.

Definition 5.2
.

For
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
) and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
be the category of dividing covers
g\colon V\to X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
such that
g^{-1}(X\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n})\to X\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n}
is an isomorphism.

We need the following basic result since we often need to check that two morphisms of fs log schemes are equal.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
).
Then the log structure map
\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}
is injective.
Furthermore,
for every fs log scheme
Y
,
the map

\Hom(X,Y)\to\Hom(\underline{X},\underline{Y}),\text{ }f\mapsto\underline{f}

is injective.

Proof.

If
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
or
S
is normal, then the log structure on
X
is the compactifying log structure by
[
52
, Proposition III.1.7.3(4)]
,
so the log structure map
\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}
is injective by definition, and
[
52
, Proposition III.1.6.2]
implies the other claim.

For general
S
,
let
f_{1},f_{2}\colon X\rightrightarrows Y
be two morphisms such that
\underline{f_{1}}=\underline{f_{2}}
.
We have the induced commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{i*}\mathcal{M}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{i*}\mathcal{O}_{X}}

for
i=1,2
. If
\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}
is injective, then the two morphisms
\mathcal{M}_{Y}\to f_{1*}\mathcal{M}_{X}
and
\mathcal{M}_{Y}\to f_{2*}\mathcal{M}_{X}
agree,
so we have
f_{1}=f_{2}
.
Hence it suffices to show that
\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}
is injective.
Equivalently, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{M}_{X,x}\to\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
is injective for every point
x\in X
.

We set
A:=\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
.
Let
P
be a neat chat of
X
at
x
in the sense of
[
52
, Definition II.2.3.1(2)]
.
Then the induced morphism
P\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X,x}
is an isomorphism,
and
P\simeq\mathbb{N}^{d}
for some integer
d
.
Let
\alpha\colon P\to A
be the log structure map.
Then we have
\alpha^{-1}(A^{*})=0
since
P\simeq\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X,x}
.
The log structure
\mathcal{M}_{X,x}
associated with
P
is
P\oplus_{\alpha^{-1}(A^{*})}A^{*}\simeq P\oplus A^{*}
, see
[
52
, (II.1.1.1)]
.
Hence it suffices to show that the induced map
\eta\colon P\oplus A^{*}\to A
is injective.
Let
e_{1},\ldots,e_{d}
be the standard coordinates in
\mathbb{N}^{d}
.
Observe that
\eta(e_{i})
is a nonzero divisor for each
i
since
X-\partial X
is dense in
X
.

Let
S^{\prime}
be the normalization of
S
,
and let
f\colon A\to B
be the map of rings whose spectrum is
\Spec(\mathcal{O}_{X,x})\times_{S}S^{\prime}\to\Spec(\mathcal{O}_{X,x})
.
Assume that

\eta(u+a_{1}e_{1}+\cdots+a_{d}e_{d})=\eta(v+b_{1}e_{1}+\cdots+b_{d}e_{d})

with
u,v\in A^{*}
and
a_{1},\ldots,a_{d},b_{1},\ldots,b_{d}\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then we have

(5.2)

f(\eta(u+a_{1}e_{1}+\cdots+a_{d}e_{d}))=f(\eta(v+b_{1}e_{1}+\cdots+b_{d}e_{d})).

The log structure on
X^{\prime}:=X\times_{S}S^{\prime}
is the compactifying log structure since
X^{\prime}\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S^{\prime}
and
S^{\prime}
is normal.
Hence the log structure map
\mathcal{M}_{X^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}
is injective.
Together with (
5.2
),
we have
a_{i}=b_{i}
for each
i
.
Since
\eta(e_{i})
is a nonzero divisor for each
i
,
we have
\eta(u)=\eta(v)
,
which implies
u=v
.
This shows that
\eta
is injective.
∎

Remark 5.4
.

A typical application of Proposition
5.3
is as follows.
Let
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
),
and let
f_{1},f_{2}\colon X\rightrightarrows Y
be two morphisms of fs log schemes.
If
j\colon U\to X
is a dense open immersion of fs log schemes such that
f_{1}j=f_{2}j
,
then we have
f_{1}=f_{2}
by Proposition
5.3
.

We are in a better situation for dividing covers as follows.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
).
Then for all dividing covers
Y,Y^{\prime}\to X
,
the map

\Hom_{X}(Y^{\prime},Y)\to\Hom_{\underline{X}}(\underline{Y^{\prime}},\underline{Y}),\text{ }f\mapsto\underline{f}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Assume
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
or
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
with normal
S
.
Then the log structure on
X
is the compactifying log structure by
[
52
, Proposition III.1.7.3(4)]
,
so
[
52
, Proposition III.1.6.2]
implies the claim, noting that we have
X-\partial X\simeq Y-\partial Y\simeq Y^{\prime}-\partial Y^{\prime}
.

For general
S
,
let
g\colon\underline{Y}\to\underline{Y^{\prime}}
be a morphism of schemes over
\underline{X}
.
By Proposition
5.3
,
it suffices to show that there exists a morphism of fs log schemes
f\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
over
X
such that
\underline{f}=g
.

Let
P\to Q,Q^{\prime}
be maps of fine monoids.
The pushout
Q\oplus_{P}^{\mathrm{int}}Q^{\prime}
in the category of integral monoids is the image of
Q\oplus_{P}^{\mathrm{mon}}Q^{\prime}\to Q^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus_{P^{\mathrm{gp}}}Q^{\prime\mathrm{gp}}
,
where
Q\oplus_{P}^{\mathrm{mon}}Q^{\prime}
denotes the pushout in the category of monoids.
Hence
\Spec(\mathbb{Z}[Q\oplus_{P}^{\mathrm{int}}Q^{\prime}])
is the closure of
\Spec(\mathbb{Z}[Q^{\mathrm{gp}}]\otimes_{\Spec[P^{\mathrm{gp}}]}\Spec[Q^{\prime\mathrm{gp}}])
in
\Spec(\mathbb{Z}[Q])\times_{\Spec(\mathbb{Z}[P])}\Spec(\mathbb{Z}[Q^{\prime}])
.
Together with the local description of dividing covers in
[
11
, Lemma A.11.3]
,
we see that the underlying scheme of the fiber product
Y\times_{X}^{\mathrm{int}}Y^{\prime}
in the category of fine log schemes is the closure of
X-\partial X\simeq(Y-\partial Y)\times_{X-\partial X}(Y^{\prime}-\partial Y^{\prime})
in
\underline{Y}\times_{\underline{X}}\underline{Y^{\prime}}
.

Now, let
g\colon\underline{Y^{\prime}}\to\underline{Y}
be a morphism of schemes over
\underline{X}
.
Then we have the graph morphism
\underline{Y^{\prime}}\to\underline{Y}\times_{\underline{X}}\underline{Y^{\prime}}
, which is a closed immersion.
Since
Y^{\prime}-\partial Y^{\prime}
is dense in
\underline{Y^{\prime}}
,
we have
\underline{Y^{\prime}}\simeq\underline{Y\times_{X}^{\mathrm{int}}Y^{\prime}}
.
Hence we only need to show that the induced map

(5.3)

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y^{\prime},y}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y\times_{X}^{\mathrm{int}}Y^{\prime},y}

is an isomorphism for every point
y\in Y^{\prime}
.

Let
s
be the image of
y
in
S
,
and let
k
be the residue field at
s
.
Since (
5.3
) is invariant under the base change from
S
to
\Spec(k)
,
it suffices to show
Y\times_{X}^{\mathrm{int}}Y^{\prime}\simeq Y^{\prime}
under the assumption
S=\Spec(k)
.

In this case,
since
S
is normal,
we already know that there exists a morphism of fs log schemes
f\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
over
X
such that
\underline{f}=g
.
Since
Y\to X
is a monomorphism,
the second projection
Y\times_{X}Y\to Y
is an isomorphism,
and hence the projection
Y\times_{X}Y^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
is an isomorphism.
It follows that the composite
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y^{\prime},y}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y\times_{X}^{\mathrm{int}}Y^{\prime},y}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y\times_{X}Y^{\prime},y}
is an isomorphism.
In particular,
the map
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y\times_{X}^{\mathrm{int}}Y^{\prime},y}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y\times_{X}Y^{\prime},y}
is surjective.
To conclude,
observe that the fiber product
Y\times_{X}Y^{\prime}
in the category of fs log schemes is the saturation
of
Y\times_{X}^{\mathrm{int}}Y^{\prime}
and hence the map
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y\times_{X}^{\mathrm{int}}Y^{\prime},y}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y\times_{X}Y^{\prime},y}
is injective.
∎

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
) and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
For
Y,Y^{\prime}\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
and
V,V^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
,

\Hom_{\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}}(Y^{\prime},Y)
and
\Hom_{\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}}(V^{\prime},V)
have at most one element.

Proof.

This is a consequence of Proposition
5.3
.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
X^{\prime}\to X
be a morphism in
\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
) and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
For
Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
and
Y^{\prime}\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X^{\prime}}^{n}
,
consider
V\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
and
V^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X^{\prime}}^{n}
obtained by (
5.1
).
If
h\colon V^{\prime}\to V
is a morphism over
X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
,
then there exists a morphism
u\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
over
X\times\square^{n}
such that the square

(5.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

commutes.

Proof.

We have the induced commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{*}\mathcal{M}_{Y^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{*}\mathcal{M}_{V^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{V^{\prime}}.}

We need to show that there exists a morphism
\mathcal{M}_{Y}\to h_{*}\mathcal{M}_{Y^{\prime}}
making the above diagram still commutative.
As a consequence of Proposition
5.3
,
the morphisms
\mathcal{M}_{Y}\to\mathcal{M}_{V}
and
h_{*}\mathcal{M}_{Y^{\prime}}\to h_{*}\mathcal{M}_{V^{\prime}}
are injective.
Hence it suffices to show that the image of
\mathcal{M}_{Y}\to h_{*}\mathcal{M}_{V^{\prime}}
is contained in the image of
h_{*}\mathcal{M}_{Y^{\prime}}\to h_{*}\mathcal{M}_{V^{\prime}}
.
Equivalently,
it suffices to show that the image of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,v}\to(h_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{V^{\prime}})_{v}
is contained in the image of
(h_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y^{\prime}})_{v}\to(h_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{V^{\prime}})_{v}
for every point
v
of
V
.
Consider the image
s
of
v
in
S
,
and let
k
be the residue field at
s
.
Since the maps
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,v}\to(h_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{V^{\prime}})_{v}
and
(h_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y^{\prime}})_{v}\to(h_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{V^{\prime}})_{v}
invariant under the base change from
S
to
\Spec(k)
,
we reduce to the case of
S=\Spec(k)
for showing this.

In this case,
we obtain (
5.4
) by
[
52
, Proposition III.1.6.2]
since the log structure on
V^{\prime}
is a compactifying log structure.
Hence the image of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,v}\to(h_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{V^{\prime}})_{v}
is contained in the image of
(h_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y^{\prime}})_{v}\to(h_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{V^{\prime}})_{v}
.
∎

Proposition 5.8
.

For
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
) and integer
n\geq 0
,
there is a fully faithful functor of categories

\rho\colon\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}

such that
\underline{Y}=\underline{\rho(Y)}
for
Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
.

Proof.

For
Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
,
consider
V
obtained by (
5.1
),
and we set
\rho(Y):=V
.
If
Y^{\prime}\to Y
is a morphism in
\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
,
then Proposition
5.5
yields a morphism
\rho(Y^{\prime})\to\rho(Y)
.

Conversely,
let
h\colon V^{\prime}:=\rho(Y^{\prime})\to V:=\rho(Y)
be a morphism in
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
.
By Lemma
5.7
,
we obtain a commutative square of the form (
5.4
).
In particular,

\Hom_{\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}}(Y^{\prime},Y)
is nonempty if and only if
\Hom_{\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}}(\rho(Y^{\prime}),\rho(Y))
is nonempty.

Together with Proposition
5.6
,
we see that for every morphism
f\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
we can uniquely assign
\rho(f)
and
\rho
satisfies the functor axioms.
∎

We refer to Definition
C.32
for the notion of strict morphisms
X\to X_{0}
when
X\in\mathrm{lSch}
and
X_{0}\in\mathrm{lSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.

Definition 5.9
.

Let
\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime}
(
\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
) be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
) spanned by those
X
admitting a strict morphism
X\to X_{0}
with
X_{0}\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.

Definition 5.10
.

For
X\in\mathrm{lSch}
,
a
fan chart of
X
is a fan
\Sigma
with a strict morphism
X\to\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}
.

Proposition 5.11
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{lSch}
with a fan chart
\Sigma
,
and let
\Sigma^{\prime}
be a subfan of
\Sigma
.
Then for every log étale monomorphism
f\colon Y\to X
such that the induced morphism
Y\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}\to X\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
is an open immersion,
there exists a subdivision of fans
\Delta\to\Sigma
such that
\Delta
contains
\Sigma^{\prime}
as a subfan and the induced morphism

f^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{T}_{\Delta}\to X\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{T}_{\Delta}

is an open immersion.
If we further assume that
f
is a dividing cover,
then
f^{\prime}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

This is a slight improvement of
[
11
, Proposition A.11.5]
.

Assume that we have shown the claim for log étale monomorphisms.
If
f
is a dividing cover,
then
f^{\prime}
is surjective by
[
11
, Proposition A.11.6]
and proper,
so
f^{\prime}
is an isomorphism.
Hence it suffices to show the claim for log étale monomorphisms.

Let
\{Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}\}
be a Zariski covering of
Y
such that we can find a desired fan
\Delta_{i}
for each
Y_{i}\to X
.
Then

\Delta:=\{\delta_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\delta_{n}:\delta_{1}\in\Delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{n}\in\Delta_{n}\}

is a desired fan for
Y\to X
.
Hence we can work Zariski locally on
Y
.
By
[
11
, Lemma A.11.3]
,
we may assume that there exists a cone
\sigma
contained in the support
\lvert\Sigma\rvert
such that
Y
is an open subscheme of
X\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{T}_{\langle\sigma\rangle}
,
where
\langle\sigma\rangle
denotes the fan with the single largest cone
\sigma
.
We may also assume that the dual monoid
\sigma^{\vee}
is a neat chart of
Y
at a point
y\in Y
.

Since the induced morphism
Y\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}\to X\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
is an open immersion,
the projection

\mathbb{T}_{\langle\sigma\rangle}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
is an open immersion too.
It follows that the fan

\{\tau\cap\sigma^{\prime}:\tau\in\langle\sigma\rangle,\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\prime}\}

is a subfan of
\Sigma^{\prime}
.
Hence the union of fans
\langle\sigma\rangle\cup\Sigma^{\prime}
is a fan.
By
[
51
, p. 18]
,
there exists a subdivision
\Delta
of
\Sigma
containing
\langle\sigma\rangle\cup\Sigma^{\prime}
as a subfan,
which is a desired fan.
∎

The following result enables us to utilize the arguments in §
4
to schemes.

Proposition 5.12
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
),
and let
X\to X_{0}
be a strict morphism with
X_{0}\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
Then for every
V\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
and integer
n\geq 0
,
there exists
V_{0}\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X_{0}}^{n}
and a morphism
X\times_{X_{0}}V_{0}\to V
in
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
.

Proof.

Let
U_{0}
be an open subscheme of
X_{0}
of the form
\mathbb{A}_{P}\times\mathbb{A}^{m}
for a smooth toric monoid
P
and integer
m\geq 0
,
and let
U:=X\times_{X_{0}}U_{0}
.
Then
U
has a chart
P
.
We set
W:=U\times_{X}V
.
Observe that the induced morphism

W\times_{U_{0}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}}((U_{0}-\partial U_{0})\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n})\to(U\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n})\times_{U_{0}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}}((U_{0}-\partial U_{0})\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n})

is an isomorphism.
Proposition
5.11
implies that
there exists a dividing cover
W_{0}\to U_{0}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
such that the induced morphism

q\colon W\times_{U_{0}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}}W_{0}\to(U\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n})\times_{U_{0}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}}W_{0}

and the projection

W_{0}\times_{U_{0}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}}((U_{0}-\partial U_{0})\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n})\to(U_{0}-\partial U_{0})\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n}

are isomorphisms.
In particular,
we have
W_{0}\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{U_{0}}^{n}
.
Compose
q^{-1}
with the projection
W\times_{U_{0}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}}W_{0}\to W
to obtain

(5.5)

U\times_{U_{0}}W_{0}\to W.

Now, let
\{U_{01},\ldots,U_{0r}\}
be a Zariski covering of
X_{0}
such that each
U_{0i}
is of the form
\mathbb{A}_{P_{i}}\times\mathbb{A}^{m_{i}}
for some smooth toric monoid
P_{i}
and integer
m_{i}\geq 0
.
Construct
W_{0i}\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{U_{0i}}^{n}
for each
U_{0i}
as the above paragraph.
Then we have a morphism
X\times_{X_{0}}W_{0i}\to V\times_{X_{0}}U_{0i}
from (
5.5
).
By Lemma
4.24
, there exists
V_{0i}\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X_{0}}^{n}
with a morphism
V_{0i}\times_{X_{0}}U_{0i}\to W_{0i}
in
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{U_{0i}}^{n}
.
Since
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X_{0}}^{n}
is cofiltered by Proposition
4.7
,
there exists
V_{0}\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X_{0}}^{n}
with morphisms
V_{0}\to V_{01},\ldots,V_{0r}
in
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X_{0}}^{n}
.
Together with Proposition
5.3
,
we can glue the induced morphisms

X\times_{X_{0}}V_{0}\times_{X_{0}}U_{0i}\to V\times_{X_{0}}U_{0i}

for all
i
to obtain a morphism
X\times_{X_{0}}V_{0}\to V
in
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
.
∎

Using this result,
we obtain some properties of
\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
and
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
as follows.

Proposition 5.13
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
).
Then the categories
\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
and
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
are cofiltered for every integer
n\geq 0
.

Proof.

Let
X\to X_{0}
be a strict morphism with
X_{0}\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
Since
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X_{0}}^{n}
is cofiltered by Proposition
4.7
,
Propositions
4.6
and
5.12
imply that
\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
and
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
are connected.
To conclude,
observe that there exists at most one morphism
Y^{\prime}\to Y
for all
Y,Y^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
by Proposition
5.6
.
∎

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
),
and let
X\to X_{0}
be a strict morphism with
X_{0}\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
Then the induced functors
\mathrm{SBl}_{X_{0}}^{n}\to\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
,
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X_{0}}^{n}\to\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
, and
\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
are cofinal for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
In particular,
for every presheaf of spectra
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S
),
we have

(5.6)

\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y_{0}\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X_{0}}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(\underline{X}\times_{\underline{X_{0}}}\underline{Y_{0}})\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(\underline{Y}).

Proof.

The categories
\mathrm{SBl}_{X_{0}}^{n}
,
\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
,
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X_{0}}^{n}
, and
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
are cofiltered by Propositions
4.7
and
5.13
.
Use Propositions
4.6
and
5.12
to show that the functors are cofinal.
The last claim is a consequence of this.
∎

Proposition 5.15
.

Let
Y\to X
be a strict morphism in
\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
).
Then the induced functor
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{Sdiv}_{Y}^{n}
is cofinal for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Let
X\to X_{0}
be a strict morphism with
X_{0}\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
Then the composite morphism
Y\to X_{0}
is strict too.
Proposition
5.14
finishes the proof.
∎

Next, we establish analogs of Construction
4.9
, Definition
4.12
, and Proposition
4.13
.

Construction 5.16
.

Let
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
be a morphism in
\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
) and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
If
g\colon V\to X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
is a dividing cover corresponding to
Y\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
,
consider the pullback
g^{\prime}\colon V^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
of
g
,
which is a dividing cover.
We have

g^{-1}((X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n})\simeq(X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n},

which implies

g^{\prime-1}((X^{\prime}-\partial X^{\prime})\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n})\simeq(X^{\prime}-\partial X^{\prime})\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{n}.

Hence we have
V^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X^{\prime}}^{n}
.
The construction of
V^{\prime}
is natural,
so we obtain a functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X^{\prime}}^{n}
given by
f^{*}(V):=V^{\prime}
.

Definition 5.17
.

Let
\mathrm{SBl}(\mathrm{Rg})
(resp.
\mathrm{SBl}(S)
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
) be the category of triples
(Y,X,[n])
with
Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
,
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S
), and
n\in\mathbb{N}
whose morphisms are of the form

(u,f,\alpha)\colon(Y^{\prime},X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}])\to(Y,X,[n])

such that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}\times\square^{n^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\square^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\times\square^{\alpha}}

commutes.

A morphism
(u,f,\alpha)\colon(Y^{\prime},X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}])\to(Y,X,[n])
is an
admissible blow-up
if
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
and
\alpha\colon[n^{\prime}]\to[n]
are isomorphisms.
Let
\mathrm{SAdm}
denote the class of admissible blow-ups.

Proposition 5.18
.

The class
\mathrm{SAdm}
in
\mathrm{SBl}(\mathrm{Rg})
(resp.
\mathrm{SBl}(S)
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
) admits calculus of right fractions.

Proof.

For the Ore condition,
let

(u,f,\alpha)\colon(Y^{\prime},X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}])\to(Y,X,[n]),\text{ }(v,\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})\colon(Z,X,[n])\to(Y,X,[n])

be morphisms in
\mathrm{SBl}(\mathrm{Rg})
(resp.
\mathrm{SBl}(S)
) so that
(v,\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})\in\mathrm{SAdm}
,
and let
X\to X_{0}
be a strict morphism with
X_{0}\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
We may assume
f=\mathrm{id}
or
\alpha=\mathrm{id}
since the pair
(f,\alpha)
is the composite
(f,\mathrm{id})\circ(\alpha,\mathrm{id})
.

Assume
\alpha=\mathrm{id}
.
Consider
W\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
corresponding to
Z\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
.
Construction
5.16
yields
W^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X^{\prime}}^{n}
with a morphism
W^{\prime}\to W
over
X\times(\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log})^{n}
.
By Proposition
5.14
,
we may assume that there exists
Z^{\prime}\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X^{\prime}}^{n}
corresponding to
W^{\prime}
.
Lemma
5.7
yields a morphism
Z^{\prime}\to Z
over
X\times\square^{n}
.
The square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}\times\square^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\square^{n}}

commutes by Proposition
5.3
.
Hence we obtain a morphism
(Z^{\prime},X^{\prime},[n])\to(Z,X,[n])
.
Since
\mathrm{SBl}_{X^{\prime}}^{n}
is cofiltered by Proposition
5.13
,
there exist morphisms
Z^{\prime\prime}\to Y^{\prime},Z^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{\prime n}
.
The square

(5.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}

commutes by Proposition
5.3
.
This shows the Ore condition for the case of
\alpha=\mathrm{id}
.

Assume
f=\mathrm{id}
.
Consider
W\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
corresponding to
Z\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
.
By Proposition
5.14
,
there exists
W_{0}\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X_{0}}^{n}
with a morphism
X\times_{X_{0}}W_{0}\to W
in
\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
.
Using Proposition
4.8
,
we may assume that there exists
Z_{0}\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X_{0}}^{n}
corresponding to
W_{0}
.
Then there exists a morphism
X\times_{X_{0}}Z_{0}\to Z
in
\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
by Proposition
5.8
.
Also, Proposition
4.13
yields a morphism
(Z_{0}^{\prime},X_{0},[n^{\prime}])\to(Z_{0},X_{0},[n])
in
\mathrm{SBl}(\mathbb{F}_{1})
.
Hence we obtain a morphism
(X\times_{X_{0}}Z_{0}^{\prime},X,[n^{\prime}])\to(Z,X,[n])
.
Since
\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n^{\prime}}
is cofiltered by Proposition
5.13
,
there exist morphisms
Z^{\prime\prime}\to Y^{\prime},X\times_{X_{0}}Z_{0}^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n^{\prime}}
.
The square (
5.7
) commutes by Proposition
5.3
.
This shows the Ore condition for the case of
f=\mathrm{id}
.

For the right cancellability condition,
let
(u_{1},f_{1},\alpha_{1}),(u_{2},f_{2},\alpha_{2})\colon(Y^{\prime},X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}])\to(Y,X,[n])
be two morphisms, and let
(v,\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})\colon(Y^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}])\to(Y^{\prime},X^{\prime},[n^{\prime}])
be an admissible blow-up such that

(u_{1},f_{1},\alpha_{1})\circ(v,\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})=(u_{2},f_{2},\alpha_{2})\circ(v,\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id}).

Then we have
f_{1}=f_{2}
and
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}
,
and we also have
u_{1}=u_{2}
by Proposition
5.3
.
This shows the right cancellability condition.
∎

Proposition 5.19
.

There are equivalence of categories

\displaystyle\mathrm{SBl}(\mathrm{Rg})[\mathrm{SAdm}^{-1}]\simeq\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{\Delta},

\displaystyle\mathrm{SBl}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S)[\mathrm{SAdm}^{-1}]\simeq\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S\times\mathbf{\Delta}.

Proof.

Argue as in Proposition
4.14
.
∎

Definition 5.20
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a presheaf of spectra on
\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
).
Argue as in Definition
4.15
but use Propositions
5.18
and
5.19
instead of Propositions
4.13
and
4.14
to obtain

\widetilde{\log}_{\bullet}\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{RglRg}\times\mathbf{\Delta},\mathrm{Sp})\text{ (resp.\ $\widetilde{\log}_{\bullet}\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S\times\mathbf{\Delta},\mathrm{Sp})$).}

Let
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}_{n}\mathcal{F}(X)
denote its value at
X
and
n\in\mathbf{\Delta}
.
Then we have

\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}_{n}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(Y)

for
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
) and integer
n\geq 0
.
We define

(5.8)

\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}:=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}_{n}\mathcal{F}\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(Y).

The construction of
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}
is natural in
\mathcal{F}
,
so we obtain the functor

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{RglRg},\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime},\mathrm{Sp})

(resp.
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S,\mathrm{Sp})
).

Definition 5.21
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
.
The
logarithm functor
is

\displaystyle\mathrm{\ell og}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\omega^{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{Rg},\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime},\mathrm{Sp})

\displaystyle\text{(resp.\ $\mathrm{\ell og}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\omega^{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{Sm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S,\mathrm{Sp})$)},

see Construction
2.18
for
\omega^{\sharp}
.
Explicitly,
for a presheaf
\mathcal{F}
of spectra on
\mathrm{Rg}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sm}/S
) and
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S
),
(
5.8
) yields a natural isomorphism

(5.9)

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(\underline{Y}).

If
X\to X_{0}
is a strict morphism with
X_{0}\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
,
then Proposition
5.14
yields a natural isomorphism

(5.10)

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y_{0}\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X_{0}}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(\underline{X}\times_{\underline{X_{0}}}\underline{Y_{0}}).

Observe that if
X_{0}=\mathbb{A}_{P}
for some sharp fs monoid
P
, then
P
is free since
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
, so we have

(5.11)

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y_{0}\in\mathrm{Adm}_{\mathbb{A}_{P}\times\square^{n}}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(\underline{X}\times_{\underline{\mathbb{A}_{P}}}\underline{Y_{0}})

together with Lemma
4.27
.

Proposition 5.22
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
).
For all integers
n\geq 0
and
1\leq i\leq n
,
there exists a functor
\mu_{i}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}\to\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n+1}
satisfying the following property:
For
V\in\mathrm{Sdiv}_{X}^{n}
,
there exists a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{*}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mu_{i}}}

Proof.

Argue as in Proposition
4.18
.
∎

Definition 5.23
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a presheaf of complexes on
\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
).
Argue as in Definition
4.20
but use Proposition
5.22
also to obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}_{\bullet}\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{RglRg}\times\mathrm{ECube},\mathrm{Sp})

(resp.
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}_{\bullet}\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S\times\mathrm{ECube},\mathrm{Sp})
)

such that

\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}_{n}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}_{n}\mathcal{F}(X)

for all
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
) and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}
be the presheaf of complexes given by

\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}(X):=\lvert\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}_{\bullet}\mathcal{F}(X)\rvert_{\mathrm{cube}}\simeq\big\lvert\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(Y)\big\rvert_{\mathrm{cube}}

for
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
).
The construction of
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}
is natural in
\mathcal{F}
,
so we obtain the functor

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{RglRg},\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime},\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z}))

(resp.
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S,\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z}))
).

The
cubical logarithm functor
is

\displaystyle\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}\omega^{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{Rg},\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime},\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z}))

(resp.
\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}^{\mathrm{cube}}\omega^{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{Sm}/S,\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S,\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z}))
).

Proposition 5.24
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a presheaf of spectra on
\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
).
If
\mathcal{F}
is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant,
then
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}
on
\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S
) is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant.
If
\mathcal{F}
is a Zariski (resp. strict Nisnevich) sheaf,
then
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}
on
\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S
) is a Zariski (resp. strict Nisnevich) sheaf.

Proof.

Argue as in Proposition
4.26
but use Proposition
5.15
also.
∎

Definition 5.25
.

Every
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
) admits a Zariski covering
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
with finite
I
such that
U_{i}\in\mathrm{RglRg}^{\prime}
(resp.
U_{i}\in\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S
) since
X
has a chart Zariski locally.
As a consequence of Proposition
5.24
,
we have the induced functors

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Zar}}(\mathrm{RglRg},\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Zar}}(\mathrm{RglRg},\mathrm{Sp}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Zar}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Zar}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{\ell og}\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Zar}}(\mathrm{Rg},\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Zar}}(\mathrm{RglRg},\mathrm{Sp}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{\ell og}\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Zar}}(\mathrm{Sm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Zar}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})

and similarly for the strict Nisnevich topology and cubical logarithm functor.

Definition 5.26
.

A Zariski sheaf
\mathcal{F}
of spectra on
\mathrm{Rg}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
) is
logarithmic
if

\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X\times\square^{n})

for
X\in\mathrm{Rg}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/S
) and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proposition 5.27
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
,
and let
\mathcal{F}
be a dividing invariant strict Nisnevich sheaf of spectra on
\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
such that the induced morphism

\mathcal{F}(X)\to\mathcal{F}(X\times\square^{n})

is an isomorphism for every
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/S
and integer
n\geq 0
.
Then we have
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)
.

Proof.

We need to show that the induced morphism

\mathcal{F}(Y)\to\mathcal{F}(Y\times\square)

is an isomorphism for every
Y\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
.
We proceed by induction on
d:=\max_{y\in Y}\rank\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,y}^{\mathrm{gp}}
.
The question is Zariski local on
Y
,
so we may assume that
Y
admits a chart
\mathbb{N}^{d}
for some
d\in\mathbb{N}
.
The claim is trivial if
d=0
,
so assume
d>0
.

Consider the strict Nisnevich distinguished squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime\prime}-W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y-W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y,}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime\prime}-W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}-W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}

with
W\in\mathrm{Sm}/S
and
Y,Y^{\prime},Y^{\prime\prime}\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S

in
[
53
, Proof of Proposition 2.4.3]
.
Note that we have
Y^{\prime}\simeq W\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}
.
By induction,
the induced morphisms

\mathcal{F}(Y-W)\to\mathcal{F}((Y-W)\times\square),\text{ }\mathcal{F}(Y^{\prime\prime}-W^{\prime\prime})\to\mathcal{F}((Y^{\prime\prime}-W^{\prime\prime})\times\square)

are isomorphisms.
By strict Nisnevich descent,
the induced morphism

\mathcal{F}(Y)\to\mathcal{F}(Y\times\square)

is an isomorphism if and only if
the induced morphism

\mathcal{F}(Y^{\prime\prime})\to\mathcal{F}(Y^{\prime\prime}\times\square)

is an isomorphism.
Arguing similarly for
Y^{\prime}
and
Y^{\prime\prime}
,
we only need to show that the induced morphism

\mathcal{F}(Y^{\prime})\to\mathcal{F}(Y^{\prime}\times\square)

is an isomorphism.

Consider the Zariski covering
\{\mathbb{A}^{1},\mathbb{A}{N}\}
of
\square
.
Using cartesian products,
we obtain the Zariski covering of
\square^{d}
consisting of
2^{d}
open subschemes.
One of them is
U:=\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}
,
and let
V
be the union of the other open subschemes.
Using the assumption on
\mathcal{F}
,
we have

\mathcal{F}(W\times\square^{n})\simeq\mathcal{F}(W\times\square^{n}\times\square).

On the other hand,
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(W\times V)\simeq\mathcal{F}(W\times V\times\square),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(W\times(U\cap V))\simeq\mathcal{F}(W\times(U\cap V)\times\square)

by induction.
Combine these three isomorphisms above and use the assumption that
\mathcal{F}
is a Zariski sheaf to have

\mathcal{F}(W\times U)\simeq\mathcal{F}(W\times U\times\square).

To conclude, observe that
W\times U\simeq Y^{\prime}
.
∎

Proposition 5.28
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
,
and let
\mathcal{F}
be a Nisnevich sheaf of spectra on
\mathrm{Sm}/S
.
If
\mathcal{F}
is logarithmic,
then we have
\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)
.

Proof.

Proposition
5.24
implies that
\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}
is a strict Nisnevich sheaf.
By Proposition
5.27
and the assumption that
\mathcal{F}
is logarithmic,
it suffices to show that
\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}
is a dividing invariant,
i.e.,
for every dividing cover
X^{\prime}\to X
in
\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
,
the induced morphism
\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X)\to\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X^{\prime})
is an isomorphism.
We can work Zariski locally on
X
,
so we may assume that
X
has a fan chart
\Sigma
.
We set
X_{0}:=\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}
.
Using
[
11
, Proposition A.11.5, Lemma C.2.1]
(with
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}:=\mathrm{Sp}
instead of a
1
-category),
we reduce to the case where
X^{\prime}\simeq X\times_{X_{0}}X_{0}^{\prime}
for some dividing cover
X_{0}^{\prime}\to X_{0}
.
Argue as in Proposition
4.23
but use Proposition
5.14
also to show

\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y_{0}\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X_{0}}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(\underline{X}\times_{\underline{X_{0}}}\underline{Y_{0}})\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y_{0}^{\prime}\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X_{0}^{\prime}}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(\underline{X}\times_{\underline{X_{0}}}\underline{Y_{0}^{\prime}}).

We conclude by (
5.6
).
∎

Definition 5.29
.

For
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
,
we denote by
\mathrm{Sh}_{\ell\mathrm{og}}(S)
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(\mathrm{Sm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})
spanned by the logarithmic sheaves,
and we have its
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-stabilization
\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\ell\mathrm{og}}(S))
.
By Proposition
5.28
, we have the induced
logarithm functor

\mathrm{\ell og}\colon\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\ell\mathrm{og}}(S))\to\mathrm{logSH}(S).

An object of
\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(\mathrm{Sm}/S,\mathrm{Sp}))
is
logarithmic
if it belongs to
\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\ell\mathrm{og}}(S))
.

Remark 5.30
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
,
and let
Z\to X
be a closed immersion in
\mathrm{Sm}/S
.
We have the induced cartesian square
