For
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Sh}_{\ell\mathrm{og}}(S)
,

\mathcal{F}(Q)\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(Q)
is cocartesian by
[
11
, Theorem 7.3.3]
.

Consider the
\infty
-category

\mathrm{MS}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(S):=\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{ebu,Nis}}(\mathrm{Sm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})),

which is a Nisnevich variant of
\mathrm{MS}
due to Annala-Hoyois-Iwasa
[
2
, §4]
,
and see
[
2
, Definition 2.1]
for
\mathrm{ebu}
.
Then
\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\ell\mathrm{og}}(S))
is a full subcategory of
\mathrm{MS}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(S)
due to the above paragraph.

Note that we have the functor

\omega^{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{MS}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(S)\to\mathrm{logSH}(S)

sending
\Sigma^{p,q}\Sigma_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\infty}X_{+}
to
\Sigma^{p,q}\Sigma_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\infty}X_{+}
for
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/S
and integers
p
and
q
.

Question 5.31
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
.
Then do we have an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\ell\mathrm{og}}(S))\simeq\mathrm{MS}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(S)?

See Conjecture
7.17
for what we expect in this direction.

6.
Logarithm of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant sheaves

In this section,
we explore what happens if we apply the logarithm functor
\mathrm{\ell og}
to logarithmic
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant sheaves of spectra.
See Theorem
6.1
for the smooth log smooth case and Theorem
6.7
for the regular log regular case.

Recall that for
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
,
we have the adjoint functors

\omega_{\sharp}:\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)\rightleftarrows\mathrm{SH}_{S^{1}}(S):\omega^{*}

such that
\omega_{\sharp}(\Sigma^{n}\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}X_{+})\simeq\Sigma^{n}\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}(X-\partial X)_{+}
for
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
and integer
n
.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
and
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{SH}_{S^{1}}(S)
.
If
\mathcal{F}
is logarithmic,
then there is a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}\simeq\omega^{*}\mathcal{F}

in
\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)
.
In particular, for every
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
,
there is a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathcal{F}(X-\partial X).

Proof.

By Propositions
5.24
and
5.28
,
we see that
\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)
is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant.
Hence by
[
53
, Proposition 2.5.7]
,
we see that the induced morphism
\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X)\to\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X-\partial X)
is an isomorphism.
To conclude,
observe that the canonical morphism
\mathcal{F}(X-\partial X)\to\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X-\partial X)
is an isomorphism since
\mathcal{F}
is logarithmic and
X-\partial X
has the trivial log structure.
∎

For
X\in\mathrm{lSch}
,
recall that a
vector bundle
\mathcal{E}\to X
is a strict morphism such that
\underline{\mathcal{E}}\to\underline{X}
is a vector bundle.
We have the
Thom space of
\mathcal{E}\to X
given by the quotient notation
\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{E}):=\mathcal{E}/(\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}\mathcal{E},E)
, where
Z
is the zero section, and
E
is the exceptional divisor on
\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}\mathcal{E}
.
If
\mathcal{F}
is a presheaf of spectra on a category containing the induced morphism
(\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}\mathcal{E},E)\to\mathcal{E}
,
then we set

\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{E})):=\fib(\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{E})\to\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}\mathcal{E},E)).

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a
(\square,\mathrm{div})
-invariant strict Nisnevich sheaf on
\mathrm{RglRg}
,
and let
\mathcal{E}\to X
be a vector bundle with
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
.
Consider the
0
-section
Z
of
\mathcal{E}
.
Then there is a natural isomorphism

\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{E}))\simeq\fib(\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}))\to\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}))).

Proof.

Consider
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(\underline{X})
given by

\mathcal{G}(Y):=\mathcal{F}(Y\times_{\underline{X}}X)

for
Y\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\underline{X}
.
Observe that
\underline{\mathcal{E}}\to\underline{X}
is a vector bundle.
By
[
11
, Proposition 7.4.5]
,
we have a natural isomorphism

\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{Th}(\underline{\mathcal{E}})\simeq\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\underline{\mathcal{E}}\oplus\mathcal{O})/\mathbb{P}(\underline{\mathcal{E}})

in
\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(\underline{X})
.
Apply
\hom_{\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(\underline{X})}(-,\mathcal{G})
to this isomorphism to conclude.
∎

Let
Z\to X
be a strict closed immersion in
\mathrm{lSch}
.
Recall that the
blow-up of
X
along
Z
is

\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}X:=\mathrm{Bl}_{\underline{Z}}\underline{X}\times_{\underline{X}}X.

The
normal bundle of
Z
in
X
is

\mathrm{N}_{Z}X:=\mathrm{N}_{\underline{Z}}\underline{X}\times_{\underline{Z}}Z.

Definition 6.3
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{Rg}
(resp.
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
),
and let
Z_{1}+\cdots+Z_{n}
be a strict normal crossing divisor on
X
.
If
Y:=(X,Z_{1}+\cdots+Z_{n-1})
and
Z:=(Z_{n},Z_{1}\cap Z_{n}+\cdots+Z_{n-1}\cap Z_{n})
,
then we will use the convenient notation

(Y,Z):=(X,Z_{1}+\cdots+Z_{n}).

Construction 6.4
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
,
and let
Z
be an effective Cartier divisor on
X
such that
(X,Z)\in\mathrm{RglRg}
.
The
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-deformation space
is

\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X:=\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}(X\times\mathbb{A}^{1})-\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}(X\times\{0\}).

Then we have the induced commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X,Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X,Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{N}_{Z}X.}

See
[
11
, Definition 7.5.1]
for the version using
\square
instead of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.

Construction 6.5
.

Let
\Sigma\to\mathbb{A}^{d+1}\times(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{n}
be a subdivision of smooth fans in the lattice
\mathbb{Z}^{d+n+1}
with
d,n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathrm{Cone}(e_{d+2},\ldots,e_{d+n+1})\in\Sigma
,
where
e_{1},\ldots,e_{d+n+1}
denotes the standard coordinates in
\mathbb{Z}^{d+n+1}
.
Let
\Sigma_{0}
be the restriction of
\Sigma
to the lattice
0\times\mathbb{Z}^{d+n}
,
which is smooth.
Then we have

\mathrm{Cone}(e_{d+2},\ldots,e_{d+n+1})\in\Sigma_{0}.

Also,
\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\Sigma_{0}
is a subdivision of
\mathbb{A}^{d+1}\times(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{n}
,
and
\Sigma
and
\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\Sigma_{0}
contain
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\Sigma_{0}
as a common subfan.
Furthermore, we have

\mathrm{Cone}(e_{d+2},\ldots,e_{d+n+1})\in\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\Sigma_{0}.

Consider the fan

\Delta:=\{\sigma\cap\sigma^{\prime}:\sigma\in\Sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\Sigma_{0}\}.

Then
\Delta
contains
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\Sigma_{0}
as a subfan,
and we have

\mathrm{Cone}(e_{d+2},\ldots,e_{d+n+1})\in\Delta.

By Proposition
D.1
,
there exists a subdivision
\Gamma\to\Delta
such that
\Gamma
is smooth and for every smooth cone
\delta
of
\Delta
,
we have
\delta\in\Gamma
.
Then
\Gamma
contains
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\Sigma_{0}
as a subfan,
and we have
\mathrm{Cone}(e_{d+2},\ldots,e_{d+n+1})\in\Gamma
.

We can interpret this in terms of standard blow-ups as follows.
For
d,n\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
\mathcal{D}_{d,n}
be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}{N}^{d+1}}^{n}
spanned by those
Y^{\prime}
satisfying the following condition: There exists
Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}}^{n}
with a morphism
p\colon Y^{\prime}\to\mathbb{A}{N}\times Y
in
\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}{N}^{d+1}}^{n}
such that
p
is an isomorphism on
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times Y
,
i.e.,
p^{-1}(\mathbb{G}_{m}\times Y)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}\times Y
is an isomorphism.
Then
\mathcal{D}_{d,n}
is cofinal in
\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}{N}^{d+1}}^{n}
.
Here,
Y
and
Y^{\prime}
correspond to
\Sigma_{0}
and
\Gamma
, the claim that
\Gamma
contains
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\Sigma_{0}
as a subfan corresponds to the claim that
p
is an isomorphism on
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times Y
,
and the claim that
Y\to\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}\times\square^{n}
and
Y^{\prime}\to\mathbb{A}{N}^{d+1}\times\square^{n}
are admissible blow-ups corresponds to the claim that
\mathrm{Cone}(e_{d+2},\ldots,e_{d+n+1})\in\Sigma_{0},\Gamma
.
In particular,
we have a natural isomorphism

(6.1)

\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}}^{n}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{Y}}\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y^{\prime}\in\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}{N}^{d+1}}^{n}},

where
\mathcal{C}_{Y}
is the full subcategory of
\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}{N}^{d+1}}^{n}
spanned by those
Y^{\prime}
satisfying the following condition: There exists a morphism
p\colon Y^{\prime}\to\mathbb{A}{N}\times Y
in
\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}{N}^{d+1}}^{n}
such that
p
is an isomorphism on
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times Y
.

Theorem 6.6
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be an
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant cdh sheaf of spectra on
\mathrm{Sch}
.
Then for every
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
and effective divisor
Z
on
X
such that
(X,Z)\in\mathrm{RglRg}
,
the squares in the induced commutative diagram

(6.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X,Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X,Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X,Z)}

are cartesian.
In particular,
there is a natural Gysin fiber sequence

(6.3)

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X))\to\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X)\to\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X,Z).

Proof.

We focus on showing that the left square of (
6.2
) is cartesian since the proof for the right square is similar.
After that, we obtain (
6.3
).

The question is Zariski local on
X
by Proposition
6.5
,
so we may assume that the induced morphism
(X,Z)\to X
is the pullback of the induced morphism
(X_{0},Z_{0})\to X_{0}
with
X_{0}:=\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}
and
Z_{0}:=\{0\}\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}
along a strict morphism
X\to X_{0}
.
With this assumption,
we only need to show that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\ell og}_{n}\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\ell og}_{n}\mathcal{F}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\ell og}_{n}\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X,Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\ell og}_{n}\mathcal{F}(X,Z)}

is cartesian for every integer
n\geq 0
.

Form the log monoid scheme
\mathrm{D}_{Z_{0}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{0}
as we did for fs log schemes.
Explicitly,
we have an isomorphism
\mathrm{D}_{Z_{0}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{0}\simeq X_{0}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
We also have the induced strict morphism of squares

\hbox to156.67pt{\vbox to55.01pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 78.33334pt\lower-27.50394pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

Furthermore,
X_{0}
and
\mathrm{D}_{Z_{0}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{0}
are strict over
\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}
,
and
(X_{0},Z_{0})
and
(\mathrm{D}_{Z_{0}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{0},Z_{0}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
are strict over
\mathbb{A}{N}^{d+1}
.

Using Proposition
5.14
and (
6.1
),
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{\ell og}_{n}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y_{0}\in\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}}^{n}}\mathcal{F}(\underline{Y}),\text{ }\mathrm{\ell og}_{n}\mathcal{F}(X,Z)\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y_{0}\in\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}}^{n}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{Y_{0}}}\mathcal{F}(\underline{Y^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{\ell og}_{n}\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X)\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y_{0}\in\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}}^{n}}\mathcal{F}(\underline{W}),\text{ }\mathrm{\ell og}_{n}\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X,Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y_{0}\in\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}}^{n}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{Y_{0}}}\mathcal{F}(\underline{W^{\prime}}),

where the notation
\mathcal{C}_{Y_{0}}
is due to Construction
6.5
, and

\displaystyle Y:=X\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times Y_{0}),\text{ }Y^{\prime}:=(X,Z)\times_{\mathbb{A}{N}^{d+1}}Y_{0}^{\prime},

\displaystyle W:=\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}{N}^{d}}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times Y_{0}),\text{ }W^{\prime}:=(\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X,Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\times_{\mathbb{A}{N}^{d+1}}Y_{0}^{\prime}.

Hence it suffices to show that the induced square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{W})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{W^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{Y^{\prime}})}

is cartesian.
The morphism
Y_{0}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{A}{N}\times Y_{0}
is an isomorphism on
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times Y_{0}
by the definition of
\mathcal{C}_{Y_{0}}
.
It follows that the induced squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{Y\times_{X}Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{Y^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{Y^{\prime}\times_{X}Z}),}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{W})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{W\times_{\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X}(Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{W^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{W^{\prime}\times_{\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X}(Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1})})}

are cartesian since
\mathcal{F}
is a cdh sheaf.
Hence it suffices to show that the induced square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{W\times_{\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X}(Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{Y\times_{X}Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{W^{\prime}\times_{\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X}(Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\underline{Y^{\prime}\times_{X}Z})}

is cartesian.

We have the induced commutative diagram with cartesian squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Z}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Z_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Z_{0}}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{1\}\times\mathbb{A}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{X_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{D}_{Z_{0}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{d+1},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}}

where
i_{1}
is the
1
-section.
We also have

\underline{Y\times_{X}Z}\simeq\underline{Z}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{d+1}}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\underline{Y_{0}}),\text{ }\underline{W\times_{\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X}(Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1})}\simeq(\underline{Z}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\times_{\mathbb{A}^{d+1}}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\underline{Y_{0}}).

From these, we see that the morphism
\underline{Y\times_{X}Z}\to\underline{W\times_{\mathrm{D}_{Z}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X}(Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1})}
can be identified with the
1
-section
\underline{Y\times_{X}Z}\to\underline{Y\times_{X}Z}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
The same holds if
Y
and
W
are replaced by
Y^{\prime}
and
W^{\prime}
.
We conclude by the
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariance of
\mathcal{F}
.
∎

As in
[
23
, Definition 1.3.2]
,
we say that a presheaf of spectra
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathrm{Rg}
satisfies
absolute purity
if the squares in

(6.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{D}_{V}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{N}_{V}U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(U-V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{D}_{V}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}U-V\times\mathbb{A}^{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{N}_{V}U-V)}

are cartesian for every regular embedding
V\to U
in
\mathrm{Rg}
.

Theorem 6.7
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be an
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant cdh sheaf of spectra on
\mathrm{Sch}
.
If
\mathcal{F}
satisfies absolute purity and the restriction of
\mathcal{F}
to
\mathrm{Sm}/S
is logarithmic for every
S\in\mathrm{Rg}
,
then there is a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathcal{F}(X-\partial X)

for every
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the number
d
of irreducible components of
\partial X
.
If
d=0
,
then the claim holds since
\mathcal{F}
is logarithmic.
Assume that the claim holds for
X
.
For every effective divisor
Z
on
X
such that
(X,Z)\in\mathrm{RglRg}
,
we need to show the claim for
(X,Z)
.

We set
U:=X-\partial X
and
V:=Z-\partial Z
for simplicity of notation.
Since
\mathcal{F}
satisfies absolute purity,
the squares in (
6.4
) are cartesian.
We have

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathcal{F}(U)

since the claim holds for
X
.
On the other hand,
we have natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle\fib(\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X)\to\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X,Z))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\fib(\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X\oplus\mathcal{O}))\to\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X)))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\fib(\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{N}_{V}U\oplus\mathcal{O}))\to\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{N}_{V}U)))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\fib(\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{N}_{V}U)\to\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{N}_{V}U-V)),

where the first (resp. second, resp. third) isomorphism is due to Proposition
6.2
(resp. induction, resp.
[
49
, Theorem 2.23 in §3]
).
Since we have a natural morphism from (
6.2
) to (
6.4
),
we have
\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}(X,Z)\simeq\mathcal{F}(U-V)
by Theorem
6.6
,
i.e., the claim holds for
(X,Z)
.
∎

7.
Logarithmic sheaves of spectra

In this section, we show that various sheaves of spectra are logarithmic assuming
\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square_{\mathbb{C}}^{n})
for all integers
n,q\geq 0
,
whose proof is postponed to
[
55
]
.

For
X\in\mathrm{Sch}
,
let
\mathrm{K}(X)
be the Bass
K
-theory spectrum of
X
.
For an abelian group like
\pi_{i}\mathrm{K}(X)
for
X\in\mathrm{Sch}
and integer
i
,
we often regard it as the associated Eilenberg-MacLane spectrum especially when taking
\otimes
in
\mathrm{Sp}
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
X\to S
be a morphism in
\mathrm{Sch}
.
Then for every proper
Y\in\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
and integer
i
,
we have natural isomorphisms

(7.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}(X)\otimes_{\mathrm{K}(S)}\mathrm{K}(S\times Y)\simeq\mathrm{K}(X\times Y),

(7.2)

\displaystyle\pi_{i}\mathrm{K}(X)\otimes_{\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(S)}\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(S\times Y)\simeq\pi_{i}\mathrm{K}(X\times Y).

Furthermore,
if
X\in\mathrm{Rg}
,
then we have a natural isomorphism

(7.3)

\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(X)\otimes_{\mathrm{K}(S)}\mathrm{K}(S\times Y)\simeq\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(X\times Y).

Proof.

If
X\in\mathrm{Rg}
, then
\mathrm{K}(X)
is
(-1)
-connected, so we can view
\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(X)
as a
\mathrm{K}(S)
-module and hence we can define (
7.3
).

We proceed by induction on
n:=\dim Y
. The claim is trivial if
n=0
.
Assume
n>0
. If
Y^{\prime}\to Y
is the blow-up along a smooth center
Z
, then the claim holds for
Z
and
Z\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}
by induction.
Hence the claim holds for
Y
if and only if the
claim holds for
Y^{\prime}
using the regular blow-up formula for K-theory
[
65
, Théorème 2.1]
.
Since
Y
is obtained from
(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{n}
by several blow-ups and blow-downs along smooth centers by
[
71
, Theorem A]
, we reduce to the case where
Y=(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{n}
. The projective bundle formula for K-theory
[
66
, Theorem 7.3]
finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
X\to S
be a morphism in
\mathrm{Sch}
.
Then for every proper
Y\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
and integer
i
,
we have natural isomorphisms

(7.4)

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}(X)\otimes_{\mathrm{K}(S)}\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}(S\times Y)\to\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}(X\times Y),

(7.5)

\displaystyle\pi_{i}\mathrm{K}(X)\otimes_{\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(S)}\mathrm{\ell og}\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(S\times Y)\to\mathrm{\ell og}\pi_{i}\mathrm{K}(X\times Y).

Furthermore
if
X\in\mathrm{Rg}
,
then we have a natural isomorphism

(7.6)

\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(X)\otimes_{\mathrm{K}(S)}\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}(S\times Y)\to\mathrm{\ell og}\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(X\times Y).\\

Proof.

Use (
5.10
) and Lemma
7.1
.
∎

For a spectrum
A
and integer
i
,
let
\tau_{\geq i}A
and
\tau_{\leq i}A
be the truncations of
A
.
The following result shows that K-theory is logarithmic if and only if
\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}
is logarithmic.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Rg}
.
Then the following conditions are equivalent.

(1)

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}(S\times\square^{n})\simeq\mathrm{K}(S)
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

(2)

\mathrm{\ell og}\tau_{\leq i}\mathrm{K}(S\times\square^{n})\simeq\tau_{\leq i}\mathrm{K}(S)
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

(3)

\mathrm{\ell og}\pi_{i}\mathrm{K}(S\times\square^{n})\simeq\pi_{i}\mathrm{K}(S)
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

(4)

\mathrm{\ell og}\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(S\times\square^{n})\simeq\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(S)
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

By (
7.6
),
(1) implies (4).
By (
7.5
),
(4) implies (3).
From (
5.9
),
we see that
\mathrm{\ell og}\tau_{\geq i}\mathrm{K}(S\times\square^{n})
is
(i-1)
-connected.
Hence we have

\lim_{i}\mathrm{\ell og}\tau_{\geq i}\mathrm{K}(S\times\square^{n})\simeq 0.

It follows that (2) implies (1).
By induction on the degree, one can show that (2) is equivalent to (3).
∎

For a perfect field
k
,
consider Voevodsky’s stable
\infty
-category of motives
\mathrm{DM}(k)
, see
[
48
, Definition 14.2]
for the triangulated version.
For a simplicial smooth scheme
X_{\bullet}
over
k
,
we take
M(X_{\bullet}):=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{i\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}M(X_{i})
.
We have a similar definition for an ind-smooth scheme.
We have the commutative ring structure on the object
M(B\mathbb{G}_{m})
of
\mathrm{DM}(k)
induced by the multiplication
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
We have isomorphisms

M(B\mathbb{G}_{m})\simeq M(\mathbb{P}^{\infty})\simeq\bigoplus_{q=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{Z}(q)[2q]

in
\mathrm{DM}(k)
by
[
49
, Proposition 3.7 in §4]
and
[
48
, Corollary 15.5]
,
so we have a commutative ring structure on
\bigoplus_{q=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{Z}(q)[2q]
.
Using this,
we have a commutative ring structure on

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\mathbb{L}^{*}):=\bigoplus_{q=0}^{\infty}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\mathbb{Z}(q)[2q])

for
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/k
,
where
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\mathbb{Z}(q))
denotes the weight
q
motivic cohomology complex,
and
\mathbb{L}:=\mathbb{Z}(1)[2]
is the notation for the Lefschetz motive.
Observe that
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\mathbb{L}^{*})
is
(-1)
-connected by
[
48
, Vanishing Theorem 19.3]
.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/k
,
where
k
is a perfect field.
Then for every proper
Y\in\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
and integer
i
,
we have natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\mathbb{L}^{*})\otimes_{R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(k,\mathbb{L}^{*})}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(Y_{k},\mathbb{L}^{*})\simeq R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X\times Y,\mathbb{L}^{*}),

\displaystyle\pi_{i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\mathbb{L}^{*})\otimes_{R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(k,\mathbb{L}^{*})}\pi_{0}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(Y_{k},\mathbb{L}^{*})\simeq\pi_{i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X\times Y,\mathbb{L}^{*}),

\displaystyle\pi_{0}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\mathbb{L}^{*})\otimes_{R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(k,\mathbb{L}^{*})}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(Y_{k},\mathbb{L}^{*})\simeq\pi_{0}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X\times Y,\mathbb{L}^{*}),

where
Y_{k}:=\Spec(k)\times Y
.

Proof.

Argue as in Lemma
7.1
,
but use the projective bundle formula
[
48
, Theorem 15.12]
and blow-up formula
[
48
, Corollary 15.13]
in
\mathrm{DM}(k)
instead.
∎

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/k
, where
k
is a perfect field.
Then the following conditions are equivalent.

(1)

\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X\times\square^{n},\mathbb{Z}(q))\simeq R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\mathbb{Z}(q))
for all
n,q\in\mathbb{N}
.

(2)

\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\tau_{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X\times\square^{n},\mathbb{Z}(q))\simeq\tau_{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\mathbb{Z}(q))
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
and
n,q\in\mathbb{N}
.

(3)

\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}H_{\mathrm{mot}}^{i}(X\times\square^{n},\mathbb{Z}(q))\simeq H_{\mathrm{mot}}^{i}(X,\mathbb{Z}(q))
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
and
n,q\in\mathbb{N}
.

(4)

\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(X\times\square^{n})\simeq\mathrm{CH}^{q}(X)
for all
n,q\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Argue as in Lemmas
7.2
and
7.3
for
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(-,\mathbb{L}^{*})
, but use Lemma
7.4
instead.
∎

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}
be a simplicial spectrum.
If there exists
d\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\mathcal{F}_{n}
is
(d-n-1)
-connected for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
then the geometric realization
\lvert\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}\rvert:=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}_{n}
is
(d-1)
-connected.

Proof.

Recall from
[
26
, Theorem X.2.9]
that we have the strongly convergent skeleton spectral sequence

E_{p,q}^{2}=H_{p}(\pi_{q}\mathcal{F}_{\bullet})\Rightarrow\pi_{p+q}\lvert\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}\rvert.

The assumption on
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}
implies that
E_{p,q}^{2}=0
whenever
p+q<d
.
We obtain the desired claim from this.
∎

The proof of the following theorem occupies
[
55
]
.
We will use this as a black box in this paper.
For a fan
\Sigma
and ring
R
,
let
\Sigma_{R}
denote the associated toric scheme over
R
.
For
Y\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
,
let
Y_{R}:=\Spec(R)\times Y
.

Theorem 7.7
.

We have
\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square_{\mathbb{C}}^{r})
for all integers
q,r\geq 0
.

Remark 7.8
.

Let us give an explicit description of
\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square_{\mathbb{C}}^{r})
.
Consider the category
\mathcal{C}_{n,r}
of smooth subdivisions
\Sigma\to(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{n+r}
satisfying the following two conditions:

(i)

If
a:=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n+r})
is a ray of
\Sigma
such that
a_{i}>0
for some
n+1\leq i\leq n+r
,
then we have
a=e_{i}
.

(ii)

\mathrm{Cone}(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})\in\Sigma
.

Note that there is an equivalence of categories

\mathcal{C}_{n,r}\simeq\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}

by Example
4.5
.

Let
e_{1},\ldots,e_{n+r}
be the standard coordinates in
\mathbb{Z}^{n+r}
.
For integers
1\leq i\leq n
and
\Sigma\in\mathcal{C}_{n,r}
,
let
D_{i,0}(\Sigma)
be the fan
V(e_{i})
,
and let
D_{i,1}(\Sigma)
be the fan consisting of the cones of
\Sigma
contained in the lattice
\mathbb{Z}^{i-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{Z}^{n+r-i}
.
Observe that
D_{i,0}(\Sigma)_{\mathbb{C}}
and
D_{i,1}(\Sigma)_{\mathbb{C}}
are closed subschemes of
\Sigma_{\mathbb{C}}
.
By Example
4.22
, the map

\delta_{i,0}^{*}\colon\mathrm{\ell og}_{n}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square{C}^{r})\to\mathrm{\ell og}_{n-1}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square{C}^{r})

agrees with the induced map

\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\Sigma\in\mathcal{C}_{r,n}}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\Sigma_{\mathbb{C}})\to\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\Sigma\in\mathcal{C}_{r,n}}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(D_{i,0}(\Sigma)_{\mathbb{C}}),

and the map

\delta_{i,1}^{*}\colon\mathrm{\ell og}_{n}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square{C}^{r})\to\mathrm{\ell og}_{n-1}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square{C}^{r})

agrees with the induced map

\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\Sigma\in\mathcal{C}_{r,n}}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\Sigma_{\mathbb{C}})\to\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\Sigma\in\mathcal{C}_{r,n}}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(D_{i,1}(\Sigma)_{\mathbb{C}}).

We also have the maps

\displaystyle p_{i}^{*}\colon\mathrm{\ell og}_{n-1}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square{C}^{r})\to\mathrm{\ell og}_{n}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square{C}^{r})\text{ for $1\leq i\leq n$},

\displaystyle\mu_{i}^{*}\colon\mathrm{\ell og}_{n-1}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square{C}^{r})\to\mathrm{\ell og}_{n}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square{C}^{r})\text{ for $1\leq i\leq n-1$},

and the maps
\delta_{i,0}^{*}
,
\delta_{i,1}^{*}
,
p_{i}^{*}
, and
\mu_{i}^{*}
form an extended cubical abelian groups.
Now,
\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square_{\mathbb{C}}^{r})
is the complex

\cdots\xrightarrow{\delta^{*}}\mathrm{\ell og}_{1}^{\flat}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square{C}^{r})\xrightarrow{\delta^{*}}\mathrm{\ell og}_{0}^{\flat}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square{C}^{r}),

where

\mathrm{\ell og}_{n}^{\flat}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square{C}^{r}):=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}\ker(\delta_{i,0}^{*})

for
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
and
\delta^{*}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{i}\delta_{i,1}^{*}
.
We will use this explicit combinatorial description of
\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square_{\mathbb{C}}^{r})
in
[
55
]
.

Theorem 7.9
.

Let
k
be a perfect field.
Then the Nisnevich sheaf
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(-,\mathbb{Z}(q))
on
\mathrm{Sm}/k
is logarithmic for every integer
q\geq 0
.

Proof.

Let
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/k
.
Consider the Zariski sheaf of complexes
\mathcal{F}_{X}
on
\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
given by
\mathcal{F}_{X}(Y):=R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X\times Y,\mathbb{L}^{*})
.
Proposition
4.30
yields a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}_{X}(Y)\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathcal{F}_{X}(Y).

Together with (
5.6
),
we have a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{\ell og}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X\times Y,\mathbb{L}^{*})\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X\times Y,\mathbb{L}^{*})

for all
Y\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
Hence it suffices to show

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\mathbb{L}^{*})\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X\times\square^{n},\mathbb{L}^{*})

for all integers
n\geq 0
.
Argue as in Lemma
7.2
but use Lemma
7.4
to show

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\mathbb{L}^{*})\otimes_{R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(k,\mathbb{L}^{*})}\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(\square_{k}^{n},\mathbb{L}^{*})\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(X\times\square^{n},\mathbb{L}^{*}).

Hence it suffices to show

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(k,\mathbb{Z}(q))\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(\square_{k}^{n},\mathbb{Z}(q))

for every integer
q\geq 0
.
By Lemma
7.5
,
it suffices to show
\mathrm{CH}^{q}(k)\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}^{\mathrm{cube}}\mathrm{CH}^{q}(\square_{k}^{n})
.
For every
V\in\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
,
\mathrm{CH}^{q}(V_{k})
is independent of the choice of the field
k
by
[
29
, Proposition 2.1]
.
Hence we may assume
k=\mathbb{C}
,
and then Theorem
7.7
finishes the proof.
∎

Theorem 7.10
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
.
Then the Nisnevich sheaf
\mathrm{K}
of spectra on
\mathrm{Sm}/S
is logarithmic.

Proof.

By (
7.4
),
it suffices to show
\mathrm{K}(\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}(\square_{\mathbb{Z}}^{n})
for every integer
n\geq 0
.
By Lemma
7.3
,
it suffices to show
\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(\square_{\mathbb{Z}}^{n})
.
Since
\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(\mathbb{Z})\simeq\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathbb{Z}
,
(
7.5
) implies
\mathrm{\ell og}\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(\square{Z}^{n})\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(\square{C}^{n})
.
Hence it suffices to show
\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\pi_{0}\mathrm{K}(\square_{\mathbb{C}}^{n})
.
By Lemma
7.3
again,
it suffices to show
\mathrm{K}(\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}(\square_{\mathbb{C}}^{n})
.

Consider the slice filtration
\mathrm{f}_{\bullet}\mathrm{K}
of
\mathrm{K}\in\mathrm{SH}(\mathbb{C})
in
[
69
, §7.1]
.
By
[
43
, Theorem 7.1.1]
,
this agrees with the Friedlander-Suslin filtration on
\mathrm{K}
obtained by
[
27
, Proposition 13.1]
since the homotopy coniveau filtration is an evident extension of the Friedlander-Suslin filtration, see
[
43
, p. 217]
.
The graded pieces
\mathrm{s}_{d}\mathrm{K}
are isomorphic to
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(-,(d)[2d])
for all integers
d
by
[
43
, Theorem 6.4.2]
.

Consider
\mathrm{f}^{d}\mathrm{K}:=\fib(\mathrm{K}\to\mathrm{f}_{d-1}\mathrm{K})
for
d\in\mathbb{Z}
.
By
[
27
, Lemma 13.5]
,

\mathrm{f}_{d}\mathrm{K}(V)
is
(d-\dim V-1)
-connected for
V\in\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{C}
.
Hence for
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/\mathbb{C}
(see Definition
5.9
for this notation) and integer
n\geq 0
,
(
5.9
) implies that
\mathrm{\ell og}_{n}\mathrm{f}_{d}\mathrm{K}(X)
is
(d-\dim X-n-1)
-connected.
Together with Lemma
7.6
,
we see that
\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{f}_{d}\mathrm{K}(X)
is
(d-\dim X-1)
-connected,
which implies

(7.7)

\lim_{d}\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{f}_{d}\mathrm{K}(X)\simeq 0.

Assume that
\mathrm{f}^{d}\mathrm{K}:=\mathrm{K}\to\mathrm{f}_{d-1}\mathrm{K}
on
\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{C}
satisfies
\mathrm{f}^{d}\mathrm{K}(\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{f}^{d}\mathrm{K}(\square_{\mathbb{C}}^{n})
for every
d\in\mathbb{Z}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
We have

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}(\square_{\mathbb{C}}^{n})\simeq\lim_{d}\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{f}^{d}\mathrm{K}(\square_{\mathbb{C}}^{n})\simeq\lim_{d}\mathrm{f}^{d}\mathrm{K}(\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathrm{K}(\mathbb{C}),

where the first isomorphism is due to (
7.7
),
and the third isomorphism is due to the completeness of the slice filtration
\mathrm{f}_{\bullet}\mathrm{K}

[
61
, Lemma 3.11]
.
It follows that we have
\mathrm{K}(\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}(\square_{\mathbb{C}}^{n})
.
Hence it suffices to show
\mathrm{f}^{d}\mathrm{K}(\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{f}^{d}\mathrm{K}(\square_{\mathbb{C}}^{n})
for every integer
d
.
By induction on degree
d
, it suffices to show that the slice
\mathrm{s}_{d}\mathrm{K}\simeq R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(-,(d)[2d])
satisfies
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(\mathbb{C},(d)[2d])\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{mot}}(\square{C}^{n},(d)[2d])
,
which is due to Theorem
7.9
.
∎

Theorem 7.11
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
.
Then we have a natural isomorphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}(X)\simeq\mathrm{K}(X-\partial X).

Proof.

The K-theory of schemes in
\mathrm{Sch}
satisfies cdh descent by
[
16
, Théorème 3.9]
and absolute purity as observed in
[
23
, Remark 6.1.3]
.
Theorems
6.7
and
7.10
finish the proof since we have a natural morphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings
\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}(X)\to\mathrm{K}(X-\partial X)
.
∎

For a scheme
X
,
let
\mathrm{THH}(X)
and
\mathrm{TC}(X)
denote the topological Hochschild homology and topological cyclic homology of
X
.

Theorem 7.12
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a
\mathrm{K}
-module object of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Zar}}(\mathrm{Sch},\mathrm{Sp})
.
Assume the following conditions:

(i)

The induced morphism

\mathrm{K}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{1})\otimes_{\mathrm{K}(X)}\mathcal{F}(X)\to\mathcal{F}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{1})

is an isomorphism for every
X\in\mathrm{Sch}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

(ii)

For every
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
and a closed immersion
Z\to X
in
\mathrm{Sm}/S
,
the induced square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}X\times_{X}Z)}

is cartesian.

Then the restriction of
\mathcal{F}
to
\mathrm{Sm}/S
is logarithmic for every
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
.
In particular,
\mathrm{THH}
and
\mathrm{TC}
are logarithmic.

Proof.

Use the conditions (i) and (ii) and argue as in Lemmas
7.1
and
7.2
to show that the induced morphism

(7.8)

\mathcal{F}(X)\otimes_{\mathrm{K}(X)}L_{\partial}\mathrm{K}(X\times Y)\to L_{\partial}\mathcal{F}(X\times Y)

is an isomorphism for every
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/S
and
Y\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
.
Since
\mathrm{K}
is logarithmic by Theorem
7.10
,
we have
L_{\partial}\mathrm{K}(X\times\square^{n})\simeq\mathrm{K}(X)
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Together with (
7.8
),
we have
L_{\partial}\mathcal{F}(X\times\square^{n})\simeq\mathcal{F}(X)
, i.e., the restriction of
\mathcal{F}
to
\mathrm{Sm}/S
is logarithmic.

The condition (i) for
\mathrm{THH}
and
\mathrm{TC}
follows from the fact that the cyclotomic trace is compatible with the projective bundle formula,
see
[
14
, Theorem 1.5]
.
The condition (ii) for
\mathrm{THH}
and
\mathrm{TC}
is a consequence of
[
11
, Theorem 7.3.3]
and the log motivic representability
[
12
, Construction 8.4.6]
or by
[
14
, Theorem 1.4]
.
∎

Recall from
[
52
, Definition III.1.2.3]
that a
log ring
(R,P)
(often called pre-log ring) is a pair of a ring
R
and a monoid
P
equipped with a map of monoids
P\to R
,
where the monoid operation on
R
is the multiplication.
A
map of log rings
is an obvious commutative square.

For an fs log scheme
X
,
let
\mathrm{logTHH}(X)
and
\mathrm{logTC}(X)
denote the topological Hochschild homology and topological cyclic homology of
X
in
[
12
, Definition 8.3.7]
,
which are obtained by globalizing Rognes’ topological Hochschild homology of log rings
[
59
, Definition 8.11]
and its cyclotomic structure in
[
42
, Definition 3]
.
Since
\mathrm{logTHH}(X)
and
\mathrm{logTC}(X)
agree with the original ones if
X
has the trivial log structure,
we have canonical isomorphisms

(7.9)

\omega_{\sharp}\mathrm{logTHH}\simeq\mathrm{THH},\text{ }\omega_{\sharp}\mathrm{logTC}\simeq\mathrm{TC}

in
\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{Sm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})
for
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
.
We refer to Construction
2.18
for

\omega_{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{PSh}(\mathrm{Sm}/S,\mathrm{Sp}).

For a ring
R
and
X\in\mathrm{lSch}/R
,
consider the Hochschild homology

\mathrm{logHH}(X/R):=\mathrm{logTHH}(X)\otimes_{\mathrm{THH}(R)}R.

If
X
has the trivial log structure,
then
\mathrm{logHH}(X/R)
agrees with the Hochschild homology
\mathrm{HH}(X/R)
.
By
[
9
, Theorem 5.15, §8.6]
,
we have the complete exhaustive HKR-filtration
\mathrm{Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathrm{HKR}}
on
\mathrm{logHH}(-/R)
whose graded pieces satisfies

(7.10)

\mathrm{gr}_{i}^{\mathrm{HKR}}\mathrm{logHH}(X/R)\simeq R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Zar}}(X,\Omega_{X/R}^{i})

for
X\in\mathrm{lSm}/R
and integer
i
.

Proposition 7.13
.

For a noetherian ring
R
,

\mathrm{HH}(-/R)\in\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Zar}}(\mathrm{Sm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})
is logarithmic.

Proof.

This is a consequence of Theorem
7.12
.
∎

Lemma 7.14
.

Let
R
be a ring,
and let
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/R
.
Then for every proper
Y\in\mathrm{Sm}/\mathbb{F}_{1}
and integer
i
,
the induced morphisms

\displaystyle\mathrm{HH}(X/R)\otimes_{R}\mathrm{HH}(Y_{R}/R)\to\mathrm{HH}(X\times Y/R),

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{\mathrm{HKR}}\mathrm{HH}(X/R)\otimes_{R}\mathrm{HH}(Y_{R}/R)\to\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{\mathrm{HKR}}\mathrm{HH}(X\times Y/R),

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{i}^{\mathrm{HKR}}\mathrm{HH}(X/R)\otimes_{R}\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{\mathrm{HKR}}\mathrm{HH}(Y_{R}/R)\to\mathrm{gr}_{i}^{\mathrm{HKR}}\mathrm{HH}(X\times Y/R)

are isomorphisms,
where
Y_{R}:=\Spec(R)\times Y
.

Proof.

Argue as in Lemma
7.1
,
but use the blow-up and projective bundle formulas for
\mathrm{HH}(-/R)
obtained by the formulas for
\mathrm{THH}
in
[
14
, Theorems 1.4, 1.5]
and the formulas for Hodge cohomology.
∎

Theorem 7.15
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
.
Then the presheaf of complexes
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Zar}}(-,\Omega_{-/S}^{i})
on
\mathrm{Sm}/S
is logarithmic for every integer
i\geq 0
.

Proof.

We can work Zariski locally on
S
,
so we may assume that
S=\Spec(R)
for some ring
R
.
Argue as in Lemma
7.3
and use Lemma
7.14
to show that the following conditions are equivalent.

(1)

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{HH}(X\times\square^{n}/R)\simeq\mathrm{HH}(X/R)
for all
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/S
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

(2)

\mathrm{\ell og}\,\mathrm{gr}_{i}^{\mathrm{HKR}}\mathrm{HH}(X\times\square^{n}/R)\simeq\mathrm{gr}_{i}^{\mathrm{HKR}}\mathrm{HH}(X)
for all
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/S
,
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

We have checked that
\mathrm{HH}(-/R)
is logarithmic, so we have (1).
Furthermore,
(2) is what we need to show due to (
7.10
).
∎

Conjecture 7.16
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Rg}
.
Then the algebraic cobordism spectrum
\mathbf{MGL}\in\mathrm{SH}(S)
due to Voevodsky
[
68
, §6.3]
is logarithmic.

Conjecture
7.16
is conceptually reasonable but technically more demanding than Theorem
7.10
.
The proof of Theorem
7.7
heavily uses known combinatorial computations of Chow groups of toric varieties.
Hence we would need to walk through more complicated computations of algebraic cobordism of toric varieties,
see
[
41
, Corollary 1.2]
for such a computation.
If we prove Conjecture
7.16
,
then the following one would be also reachable:

Conjecture 7.17
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Rg}
.
Then every
\mathbf{MGL}
-module in
\mathrm{MS}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(S)
is logarithmic.

This would indicate that there is a close relationship between the full subcategories of orientable objects of
\mathrm{MS}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(S)
and
\mathrm{logSH}(S)
.

8.
Motivic representability of logarithmic K-theory

In this section,
we show that K-theory is representable in
\mathrm{logSH}(S)
for every
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
,
see Theorem
8.13
.
We first need to set up a natural transformation
u
as follows.

Construction 8.1
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
.
Then we have the natural transformation

u\colon\mathrm{id}\to\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})

given as follows.
The morphism of spectra
u(\mathcal{F})(X)
, which we denote by
u_{\mathcal{F}}(X)
, is given by

(8.1)

\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(X)\to\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(Y)

for
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})
and
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S
,
see Definition
5.9
for
\mathrm{SmlSm}^{\prime}/S
.
Here, the colimit on the source is the constant colimit,
and when
n
is fixed,
the morphism
\mathcal{F}(X)\to\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(Y)
factors through
\mathcal{F}(X)\to\mathcal{F}(X\times\square^{n})
induced by the projection
X\times\square^{n}\to X
.

Proposition 8.2
.

Let
P
be a sharp fs monoid,

S\in\mathrm{Sch}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}^{n}
.
Then there exists a sequence of admissible blow-ups along smooth centers

Y_{m}\to\cdots\to Y_{0}:=\mathbb{A}_{P}\times\square^{n}

in
\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}^{n}
such that there is a morphism
Y_{m}\to Y
in
\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}^{n}
.

Proof.

We have
\underline{Y}\simeq\underline{\mathbb{T}_{\Delta}}

for some subdivision
\Delta
of
\Spec(\mathbb{F}_{1}[P])\times(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{n}
.
Consider the cone
\sigma:=\mathrm{Cone}(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})
of
\Spec(\mathbb{F}_{1}[P])\times(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{n}
,
where
e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}
are the standard coordinates for
(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{n}
.
The condition that
Y\to\mathbb{A}_{P}\times\square^{n}
is an admissible blow-up is equivalent to the condition that
\sigma\in\Delta
.
By Proposition
D.6
,
there exists a sequence of star subdivisions

\Delta_{m}\to\cdots\to\Delta_{0}:=\Spec(\mathbb{F}_{1}[P])\times(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{n}

such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{m}
and there is a subdivision
\Delta_{m}\to\Delta
.
Consider
Y_{i}\in\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}^{n}
for each
i
such that
\underline{Y_{i}}:=\underline{\mathbb{T}_{\Delta_{i}}}
.
Then we have a desired sequence.
∎

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
and
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)
.
Then
u_{\mathcal{F}}\colon\mathcal{F}\to\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}
is an isomorphism and hence admits an inverse
v_{\mathcal{F}}\colon\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}
.

Proof.

We can work Zariski locally on
X
by Proposition
5.24
,
so we may assume that
X
has a chart
P
such that
P
is a sharp fs monoid.
By Proposition
5.14
,
it suffices to show that the morphism

u_{\mathcal{F}}\colon\mathcal{F}(X)\to\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}^{n}}\mathcal{F}(X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}Y)

is an isomorphism for every integer
n\geq 0
.
By Proposition
8.2
,
it suffices to show that the morphism
\mathcal{F}(X)\to\mathcal{F}(X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}Y)
is an isomorphism if
Y\to\mathbb{A}_{P}\times\square^{n}
is a composite of admissible blow-ups along smooth centers.
Since
\mathcal{F}
is invariant under admissible blow-up by Theorem
2.11
and
\square
-invariant, we deduce this claim.
∎

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
.
Then
\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)
is generated under colimits by
\Sigma^{n}\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}X_{+}
for all
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/S
and integers
n
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)
generated under colimits by those elements.
It suffices to show that for every
Y\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
,
\Sigma^{\infty}Y_{+}
is in
\mathcal{C}
.
We proceed by induction on the number
d
of irreducible components of
\partial Y
.
The claim is clear if
d=0
.
Assume
d>0
.
There exists
Y_{0}\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
and its smooth divisor
Z_{0}
such that
\underline{Y_{0}}=\underline{Y}
and
\partial Y=\partial Y_{0}+Z_{0}
.
By
[
11
, Proposition 7.4.5, Theorem 7.5.4]
,
there is an isomorphism

\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}Y/Y_{0}\simeq\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{N}_{Z_{0}}Y_{0}\oplus\mathcal{O})/\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{N}_{Z_{0}}Y_{0}).

By induction,
we have
\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}Y_{0+},\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{N}_{Z_{0}}Y_{0}\oplus\mathcal{O})_{+},\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{N}_{Z_{0}}Y_{0})_{+}\in\mathcal{C}
.
It follows that we have
\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}Y_{+}\in\mathcal{C}
.
∎

Theorem 8.5
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})
.
If
\omega_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(\mathrm{Sm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})
is logarithmic and
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)
,
then there is a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{\ell og}\omega_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}\simeq\mathcal{F}

in
\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)
.
As a consequence,
for
X\in\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
,
we have a natural isomorphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings in cyclotomic spectra

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{THH}(X)\simeq\mathrm{logTHH}(X),

and natural isomorphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{TC}(X)\simeq\mathrm{logTC}(X).

Proof.

Apply
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}
to the counit
\omega^{\sharp}\omega_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}
to obtain
\mathrm{\ell og}\omega_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}\to\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}
.
By Proposition
8.3
,
we have an isomorphism
\mathcal{F}\simeq\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}
.
Also, by Proposition
8.4
, it suffices to show that the induced morphism

(8.2)

\mathrm{\ell og}\omega_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}(X)\to\mathcal{F}(X)

is an isomorphism for
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/S
.
This follows from the assumption that
\omega_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}
is logarithmic.

Let
\mathrm{CycSp}
denote the
\infty
-category of cyclotomic spectra.
The claims for
\mathcal{F}=\mathrm{THH}
follow from (
7.9
) since the morphisms

\mathrm{\ell og}\omega_{\sharp}\mathrm{logTHH}(X)\to\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathrm{logTHH}(X),\text{ }\mathrm{logTHH}(X)\to\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathrm{logTHH}(X)

are morphisms of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings in cyclotomic spectra due to the following reasons:
We used sifted colimits to form
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}
,
and the forgetful functors
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{CycSp})\to\mathrm{CycSp}\to\mathrm{Sp}
preserve sifted colimits by
[
47
, Corollary 3.2.3.2]
and
[
50
, Corollary II.1.7]
.
The proof of the claim for
\mathcal{F}=\mathrm{TC}
is similar.
∎

Next, we discuss a functorial property relating
u
and
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}
.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
.
Then the two natural transformations

u\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}},\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\circ u\colon\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\to\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S)\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S)

are homotopic.

For
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
,
we have the morphism
\mathcal{A}_{mn}\to\mathcal{C}_{mn}
induced by the composite

\mathcal{F}(Y)\to\mathcal{F}(Y\times\square^{m})\to\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{V\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{m})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{W\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{V}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(W)

for
Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
whose second morphism corresponds to
V=X\times\square^{m}
and
W=Y\times\square^{m}
.
We have the morphism
\mathcal{B}_{mn}\to\mathcal{C}_{mn}
induced by the composite

\mathcal{F}(Y)\to\mathcal{F}(Y\times\square^{n})\to\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{V\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{m})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{W\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{V}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(W)

for

Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
whose second morphism corresponds to
V=Y
and
W=Y\times\square^{n}
.

When we evaluate
u\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}
and
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\circ u
on
\mathcal{F}(X)
,
we have the morphisms of bisimplicial colimits

\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{m\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{A}_{mn}\to\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{m\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{C}_{mn},\text{ }\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{m\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{B}_{mn}\to\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{m\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{C}_{mn}.

It suffices to show that these two are naturally homotopic.
A bisimplicial colimit is isomorphic to the diagonal simplicial colimit
[
46
, Definition 5.5.8.1, Lemma 5.5.8.4]
.
Hence it suffices to show that
\mathcal{A}_{nn}\to\mathcal{C}_{nn}
and
\mathcal{B}_{nn}\to\mathcal{C}_{nn}
are naturally homotopic for
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Consider
Y\in\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n}
.
By Proposition
5.18
,
there exists a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times\square^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\square^{n},}

where
Y^{\prime}\in\mathrm{SBl}_{Y}^{n}
,
and the morphism
Y\times\square^{n}\to X\times\square^{n}
is a pullback of the composite
Y\to X\times\square^{n}\to X
.
Then the morphisms
\mathcal{A}_{nn}\to\mathcal{C}_{nn}
and
\mathcal{B}_{nn}\to\mathcal{C}_{nn}
agree since both have a factorization

\mathcal{F}(Y)\to\mathcal{F}(Y^{\prime})\to\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{V\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{m})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{W\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{V}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{F}(W)

whose second morphism corresponds to
V=Y
and
W=Y^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 8.7
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})
and
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)
,
where
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
.
If
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)
,
then the map

\alpha\colon\pi_{0}\Hom_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})}(\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\to\pi_{0}\Hom_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})

obtained by precomposing
u_{\mathcal{F}}\colon\mathcal{F}\to\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Consider the map

\beta\colon\pi_{0}\Hom_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\to\pi_{0}\Hom_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})}(\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})

sending
f\colon\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
to the composite morphism

\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}f}\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{v_{\mathcal{G}}}\mathcal{G},

see Proposition
8.3
for
v_{\mathcal{G}}
.
The composite morphism

\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{u_{\mathcal{F}}}\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}f}\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{v_{\mathcal{G}}}\mathcal{G}

is homotopic to
f
since we have a natural transformation
\mathrm{id}\to\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}
and
v_{\mathcal{G}}
is an inverse of
u_{\mathcal{G}}
.
Hence we have
\alpha(\beta(f))\simeq f
.

Let
g\colon\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
be a morphism in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})
.
We have the induced commutative diagram

(8.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{G}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\mathcal{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\mathcal{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\mathcal{G}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}u_{\mathcal{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}g}

To show
\beta(\alpha(g))\simeq g
,
we need to show that the composite

\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}u_{\mathcal{F}}}\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}g}\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{v_{\mathcal{G}}}\mathcal{G}

is homotopic to
g
.
For this,
it suffices to show
u_{\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}}\simeq\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}u_{\mathcal{F}}
using the commutativity of the right square of (
8.3
).
This is a consequence of Lemma
8.6
.
∎

For
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
,
let

L_{\mathrm{lmot}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)

denote the localization functor.
The constructions of
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}
and
L_{\mathrm{lmot}}
are different, but they are related as follows.

Theorem 8.8
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})
,
where
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
.
If
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)
,
then there exists a natural isomorphism

\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}\simeq L_{\mathrm{lmot}}\mathcal{F}

in
\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.7
,
we have a natural isomorphism

\pi_{0}\Hom_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})}(\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F},L_{\mathrm{lmot}}\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\pi_{0}\Hom_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})}(\mathcal{F},L_{\mathrm{lmot}}\mathcal{F}).

Let
p\colon\mathcal{F}\to L_{\mathrm{lmot}}\mathcal{F}
be the canonical morphism,
and this corresponds to a morphism
f\colon\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}\to L_{\mathrm{lmot}}\mathcal{F}
.

Since
\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)
and
L_{\mathrm{lmot}}
is a localization functor,
we also have a natural isomorphism

\pi_{0}\Hom_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})}(L_{\mathrm{lmot}}\mathcal{F},\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\pi_{0}\Hom_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})}(\mathcal{F},\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}).

Let
q\colon\mathcal{F}\to\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}
be the canonical morphism,
and this corresponds to a morphism
g\colon L_{\mathrm{lmot}}\mathcal{F}\to\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}
.

We have
gp\simeq q
and
fq\simeq p
.
Hence we have
fgp\simeq p
and
gfq\simeq q
.
Use the natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\Hom_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})}(\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F},\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\pi_{0}\Hom_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})}(\mathcal{F},\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathcal{F}),

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\Hom_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})}(L_{\mathrm{lmot}}\mathcal{F},L_{\mathrm{lmot}}\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\pi_{0}\Hom_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})}(\mathcal{F},L_{\mathrm{lmot}}\mathcal{F})

to show
fg\simeq\mathrm{id}
and
gf\simeq\mathrm{id}
.
Hence
f
and
g
are isomorphisms.
∎

Corollary 8.9
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(\mathrm{Sm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})
,
where
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
.
If
\mathcal{F}
is logarithmic,
then there exists a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathcal{F}\simeq L_{\mathrm{lmot}}\omega^{\sharp}\mathcal{F}.

Proof.

Combine Theorem
8.8
with Proposition
5.28
.
∎

Definition 8.10
.

For
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
,
the
log algebraic K-theory spectrum
is
\mathrm{\ell og}\mathbf{KGL}\in\mathrm{logSH}(S)
,
where

\mathbf{KGL}:=(\mathrm{K},\mathrm{K},\ldots)\in\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(\mathrm{Sm}/S,\mathrm{Sp}))

is the
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-spectrum whose bonding morphisms
\mathrm{K}\to\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mathrm{K}
are obtained by the projective bundle formula.
Observe that we have a natural isomorphism

(8.4)

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathbf{KGL}\simeq\Sigma^{2,1}\mathrm{\ell og}\mathbf{KGL}.

On the other hand,
if
S\in\mathrm{Rg}
,
then we have the definition

\mathrm{log}\mathbf{KGL}:=\omega^{*}\mathbf{KGL}\in\mathrm{logSH}(S)

in
[
12
, Definition 6.5.6]
,
where
\mathbf{KGL}\in\mathrm{SH}(S)
denotes the algebraic K-theory spectrum.

Theorem 8.11
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Rg}
.
Then we have a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathbf{KGL}\simeq\mathrm{log}\mathbf{KGL}

in
\mathrm{logSH}(S)
.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of Theorems
6.1
and
7.10
.
∎

Proposition 8.12
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a morphism in
\mathrm{Sch}
.
Then we have natural isomorphisms

f^{*}\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K},\text{ }f^{*}\mathrm{\ell og}\mathbf{KGL}\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\mathbf{KGL}.

Proof.

Consider the induced commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(\mathrm{Sm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Sp})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(\mathrm{Sm}/X,\mathrm{Sp})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/X,\mathrm{Sp})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(X).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathrm{lmot}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathrm{lmot}}}

As a consequence of
[
49
, Proposition 1.3 in §4]
(see also
[
49
, Proposition 3.14 in §4]
),
we have
f^{*}\mathrm{K}\simeq\mathrm{K}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(\mathrm{Sm}/X,\mathrm{Sp})
.
Use the above diagram and Corollary
8.9
to obtain
f^{*}\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}
in
\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)
.
From this,
we also obtain
f^{*}\mathrm{\ell og}\mathbf{KGL}\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\mathbf{KGL}
in
\mathrm{logSH}(S)
.
∎

Theorem 8.13
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
and
X\in\mathrm{Sm}/S
.
Then there are natural isomorphisms

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}(X)\simeq\hom_{\mathrm{logSH}_{S^{1}}(S)}(\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}X_{+},\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K})\simeq\hom_{\mathrm{logSH}(S)}(\Sigma_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\infty}X_{+},\mathrm{\ell og}\mathbf{KGL}).

Proof.

The second isomorphism is clear.
The first isomorphism is a consequence of Proposition
5.28
and Theorem
7.10
.
∎

Construction 8.14
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
.
By
[
12
, Theorem 5.2.10]
, we have an isomorphism

\mathbb{P}^{\infty}\simeq\mathrm{B}\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log}

in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Spc}_{*})[\square^{-1},\mathrm{SmAdm}^{-1}]
.
Using the multiplication
\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log}\times\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log}\to\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log}
in
[
12
, Example 5.2.8]
,
we have the induced morphism
\mathbb{P}^{\infty}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{\infty}\to\mathbb{P}^{\infty}
and hence a morphism

\beta\colon\mathbb{P}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{\infty}\to\mathbb{P}^{\infty}

in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{sNis}}(\mathrm{SmlSm}/S,\mathrm{Spc}_{*})[\square^{-1},\mathrm{SmAdm}^{-1}]
.
From this, we also get

\beta\colon\Sigma^{2,1}\Sigma_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty})_{+}\to\Sigma_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty})_{+}

in
\mathrm{logSH}(S)
.

The following result, which is a log motivic version of the Snaith theorem
[
62
]
, relies on a motivic version of the Snaith theorem established in
[
3
]
:

Theorem 8.15
.

For
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
,
there exists a natural isomorphism

\Sigma_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty})_{+}[\beta^{-1}]\simeq\mathrm{\ell og}\mathbf{KGL}

in
\mathrm{logSH}(S)
.

Proof.

By Proposition
8.12
,
it suffices to show the claim when
S=\Spec(\mathbb{Z})
.
In particular, we may assume
S\in\mathrm{Rg}
.
Consider the algebraic K-theory spectrum
\mathbf{KGL}
in
\mathrm{MS}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(S)
.
By
[
3
, Theorem 5.3.3]
,
we have an isomorphism

\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty})_{+}[\beta^{-1}]\simeq\mathbf{KGL}

in
\mathrm{MS}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(S)
with
\beta
in
[
3
, Lemma 5.2.6]
.
Apply
L_{\mathrm{lmot}}\omega^{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{MS}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(S)\to\mathrm{logSH}(S)
to this isomorphism and use Theorem
7.10
and Corollary
8.9
to conclude.
Here, we can see that
L_{\mathrm{lmot}}\omega^{\sharp}
sends
\beta
in
[
3
, Lemma 5.2.6]
to
\beta
in Construction
8.14
using the open immersion
\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log}
and hence the induced morphism
\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathrm{B}\mathbb{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\log}
.
∎

9.
Logarithmic cyclotomic trace

See
[
12
, Theorem 8.6.5]
for the log cyclotomic trace under resolution of singularities.
We now define the log cyclotomic trace without resolution of singularities.

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
(resp.
\mathrm{SmlSm}/S
with
S\in\mathrm{Sch}
).
Then there exists a natural morphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings

\mathrm{logTr}\colon\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}(X)\to\mathrm{logTC}(X)

that coincides with the usual cyclotomic trace when
X
has the trivial log structure.
We call
\mathrm{logTr}
the
log cyclotomic trace
.

Proof.

For every
Y\in\mathrm{lSch}
,
consider the composite morphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings

\mathrm{K}(\underline{Y})\to\mathrm{TC}(\underline{Y})\to\mathrm{logTC}(Y).

Use this to obtain the morphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings

(9.1)

\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}(X)=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{K}(\underline{Y})\to\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{Y\in(\mathrm{SBl}_{X}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{logTC}(Y)=\widetilde{\mathrm{\ell og}}\mathrm{logTC}(X).

The right-hand side is isomorphic to
\mathrm{logTC}(X)
by Proposition
8.3
.
Hence we get
\mathrm{logTr}
.

Note that by
[
47
, Corollary 3.2.3.2]
,
the colimits in (
9.1
) computed as
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings agree with the colimits in (
9.1
) computed as spectra after forgetting the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-structures.

Assume that
X
has the trivial log structure.
Then we have the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{TC}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTC}(X),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTr}}

so
\mathrm{logTr}
can be identified with
\mathrm{Tr}
.
∎

Remark 9.2
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
.
Then by Theorem
7.11
, the log cyclotomic trace can be written as

\mathrm{logTr}\colon\mathrm{K}(X-\partial X)\to\mathrm{TC}(X).

We also have a commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTC}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}(X-\partial X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTC}(X-\partial X).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}

Hence the cyclotomic trace factors through the log cyclotomic trace,
i.e., the log cyclotomic trace is a refinement of the cyclotomic trace.

Remark 9.3
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{Rg}
.
If
D
is a strict normal crossing divisor on
X
,
then let
(X,D)
denote the fs log scheme with underlying scheme
X
and the Deligne-Faltings log structure associated
D
.
By
[
52
, Example III.1.11.9]
,
we have
(X,D)\in\mathrm{RglRg}
.

If
Z\to X
is a closed immersion in
\mathrm{Rg}
such that
D+Z
is a strict normal crossing divisor on
X
,
then we use the convenient notation

((X,D),Z):=(X,D+Z).

Corollary 9.4
.

Let
i\colon Z\to X
be a closed immersion in
\mathrm{Rg}
.
Then there exists a morphism of fiber sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}(X-Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{TC}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{TC}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTC}(\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}X,E),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}}

where
E
is the exceptional divisor,

j\colon X-Z\to X
is the open immersion,
and
p\colon(\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}X,E)\to X
is the projection.

Proof.

Consider the
\square
-deformation space

\mathrm{D}_{Z}X:=\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}(X\times\square)-\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}(X\times\{0\}).

Then we have the induced commutative diagram

\mathrm{Gys}(Z\to X):=\hbox to285.18pt{\vbox to52.43pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 142.58589pt\lower-26.21309pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

where
E^{D}
and
E^{N}
are the exceptional divisors,
see
[
11
, Definition 7.5.1]
and
[
54
, Remark 2.8]
.

By
[
17
, Theorem 13.6.3]
and Theorem
7.11
,
the squares in
\mathrm{\ell og}\mathrm{K}(\mathrm{Gys}(Z\to X))
are cartesian.
Moreover, by
[
54
, Corollary 4.7]
,
the squares in
\mathrm{logTC}(\mathrm{Gys}(Z\to X))
are cartesian.
Together with Proposition
6.2
and Theorem
9.1
,
we have a morphism of fiber sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fib(\mathrm{K}(\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X\oplus\mathcal{O}))\to\mathrm{K}(\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}(X-Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fib(\mathrm{TC}(\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X\oplus\mathcal{O}))\to\mathrm{TC}(\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{TC}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTC}(\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}X,E).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}}

To conclude,
observe that the projective bundle formulas for
\mathrm{K}
and
\mathrm{TC}
are compatible via the cyclotomic trace by
[
14
, Theorem 1.5]
.
∎

See
[
9
, §2.6]
for the animated version of log rings.

Lemma 9.5
.

Let
(R,P)\to(A,M),(B,N)
be maps of animated log rings.
Then the induced morphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings in cyclotomic spectra

(9.2)

\mathrm{THH}(A,M)\otimes_{\mathrm{THH}(R,P)}\mathrm{THH}(B,N)\to\mathrm{THH}(A\otimes_{R}B,M\oplus_{P}N)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The forgetful functor from the category of cyclotomic spectra to spectra is conservative by
[
50
, Corollary II.1.7]
.
Hence it suffices to show that (
9.2
) is an isomorphism as
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings.
We have an isomorphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings

\mathrm{THH}(R,P)\simeq\mathrm{THH}(R)\otimes_{\mathrm{THH}(\mathbb{S}[P])}\mathbb{S}[P\oplus\mathrm{B}P^{\mathrm{gp}}]

by the animated versions of
[
59
, Lemma 3.17, Remark 8.12]
, see also
[
9
, Proposition 2.21]
.
Hence it suffices to show that the functors
R\mapsto\mathrm{THH}(R)
,
P\mapsto\mathrm{THH}(\mathbb{S}[P])
, and
P\mapsto\mathbb{S}[P\oplus\mathrm{B}P^{\mathrm{gp}}]
preserve colimits.
Since
\mathrm{THH}
is defined by a colimit,

R\mapsto\mathrm{THH}(R)
preserves colimit.
To conclude,
observe that
\mathbb{S}[-]
, the group completion, and the bar construction preserve colimits.
∎

Corollary 9.6
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{RglRg}
, and let
Z\to\underline{X}
be a closed immersion in
\mathrm{Rg}
such that
\partial X+Z
is a strict normal crossing divisor on
\underline{X}
.
Then there exists a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}(Z-\partial Z)}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTC}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTC}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTC}(X,Z)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTr}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTr}}

whose rows are cofiber sequences.
